  >Subject: Re: Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions & EMFs
  >"Lately Dr. Levengood has been branching out in his research.
  >According to Nancy Talbott of BLT, he recently undertook an
  >unpublicized 'personal experiment' to attempt to ascertain the
  >reality of the so-called 'alien abduction phenomenon.' Many
  >attempts have been made in the past to monitor abductees' homes
  >with video cameras, tape recorders and other gear. Invariably,
  >the electronics failed at the crucial moments.
  >Real scientists would say that alone is important evidence.
  Yes, among other apparently noteworthy items.
  I invested some time in reviewing your previous e-mails, Ms.
  White. I would be quite interested in reading any thoughts you
  might care to share about the significance of electrical
  anomalies and apparent microwave activity as it relates to
  reports of the strange. Thank you.

  Jack Brewer

                                   Re: Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions
  From: Jack Brewer <brewer.jack.nul>
  Date: Tue, 1 Jun 2010 08:44:58 -0700 (PDT)
  Archived: Wed, 02 Jun 2010 07:47:41 -0400
  Subject: Re: Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions

  >From: John Rimmer <johnrimmer.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Mon, 31 May 2010 13:09:31 +0000 (GMT)
  >Subject: Re: Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions & EMFs
  >I'm sure there is (associations between the types of phenomena).
  >I wonder what it could be?
  I interpret the question to be rhetorical and, based on your
  other replies, I interpret the question to be intended to imply
  that quite terrestrial origins are responsible. Am I correctly
  interpreting your intentions? If so, at your convenience I would
  appreciate you sharing your preferred hypotheses.

  Thank you,
  Jack Brewer

  >White. I would be quite interested in reading any thoughts you
  >might care to share about the significance of electrical
  >anomalies and apparent microwave activity as it relates to
  >reports of the strange. Thank you.
  There are different phenomena in which electrical anomalies have
  been detected, such as close proximity to UFOs, hauntings and
  poltergeist events, crop circles, and my own arena, organized
  stalking and electronic harassment (OS/EH.)
  Long time members of this forum know about organized stalking
  and electronic harassment, and if anyone is curious, I've tried
  to "sugar down" the essence of this crime in either of these two
  items:
  http://www.targcomics.net
  http://www.targcomics.net/osatv.pdf
  My estimate of the significance of electrical (a better term
  might be electromagnetic) anomalies is simple:
  We, the ordinary inhabitants of this planet, have not yet been
  schooled in those laws of physics which operate during the
  phenomena listed above. My belief is that those laws of physics
  are known to some, and being deliberately withheld, as a power
  gambit.
  While OS/EH targets can't govern when and how the electronic
  assaults happen, we get such assaults more often than people get
  close passes by UFOs or crop circle EM anomalies.
  Our members have purchased a number of EM detectors, and have
  experimented over the years. Notes on how some of the
  experiments were done, and the results (so far not of courtroom
  quality) are listed in this e-booklet:
  http://www.targcomics.net/oscd.pdf
  As a retired engineer and licenced ham radio operator for 55
  years, I can say some of the EM assaults and effects can't be
  done using the level of physics taught in today's schools. Even
  the EM readings are anomalous. Some of them emanate from a
  point.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m02-003.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:02]
Re: Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions

  In my case, for several years, over three different apartments,
  I would get strong EM noise, detectable on an AM radio,
  emanating from a point, ten inches above my pillow. In mid air,
  in other words.
  More than that, this signal filled my apartment, yet suddenly
  dropped to zero a couple of feet outside my apartment door, in
  spite of the walls and door being electromagnetically
  transparent (wooden door, non-steel-reinforced concrete block
  wall.)
  Your original question was to comment on the significance of the
  electrical anomalies. Again, I see these anomalies as proof that
  there is one _heck_ of a lot of physics that we don't know yet.
  These anomalies are ignored by the "That violates the laws of
  physics" folks because the anomalies do prove there is a lot
  more to learn, and they don't want to deal with that.

  Eleanor White

Crop Circles - Back To The Past




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 3

                                                   Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  Date: Wed, 2 Jun 2010 22:03:30 +0000
  Archived: Thu, 03 Jun 2010 07:08:17 -0400
  Subject: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  Dear Listers:
  The subject of crop circles brought back memories of the
  beginning of my interest in the phenomena.
  In 1991, I wrote a paper I called Evaluation And Discussion Of
  So-Called Crop Circles. The gist of my opinion was that they
  were sophisticated graffiti. The authors of the graffiti were
  unknown - whether terra or non-terra was unknown.
  I used my knowledge of paleolithic Southwest Amerind pictographs
  to translate the so-called insectograms manifesting near
  Stonehenge. It was a very simple fit and translation had an
  internal symbolic logic and related to the physical location.
  Just for the hell of it, I easily translated two of the
  graffiti. One read: Many people/tribes came here to hold a
  talk/communication. The other graffiti pointed to a paleolithic
  ruin located near Stonehenge. It read: Many tribes came in front
  of here and climbed up the hidden hill. I thought it was clever
  to use a paleolithic language from Southwestern U.S.A. applied
  to a paleolithic ruin in England.
  Someone recently tried to pretend they knew Mayan hieroglyphs.
  The design was very pretty, but it didn't make any sense using
  Maya hieroglyphs. I know a little about Maya hieroglyphs - using
  Thompson's translation manual to fill in the gaps in my
  knowledge.
  As we, of a scientific mind know, something either is or it
  ain't. It was a very pretty design using elements from Mayan
  pottery or fabric design. But did not translate into a coherent
  communication. The only part of the design the on-line
  'translator' could relate to were the dots and dashes. Ignoring
  the main part of the design. Which was very pretty, but conveyed
  no coherent message such as the earlier insectograms I focused
  on years ago. There was no way to translate the field graffiti.
  So, yes, there are people who like to create an enigma without
  signing their name or taking credit. Usually, one can find them
  off on the fringes laughing - behind their hand - at the
  reactions they have caused in their potential audience. Just
  think, the artists have a worldwide audience. Wow! What an ego
  trip.
  Bottomline? The unknown artists are attempting to create more
  spectacular designs to keep the audience interested. I think
  they are fun and appreciate them as art work.

  KK


  Listen to 'Strange Days... Indeed' - The PodCast
  At:
  http://www.virtuallystrange.net/ufo/sdi/program/

  Hi, List,
  After having viewed this video several times in full-screen
  format, I'm convinced this is a slowly shifting V-formation of
  Canada Geese flying over, and whose whitish under-bellies are
  reflecting ground light sources enhanced by use of the
  videographer's IR scope and cranked up digital video camera's
  zoom feature.
  If you look very closely, you can see what appears to be a
  flapping motion on each side of each object, perpendicular to
  the axis of motion or flight.
  If you read the article noted above on the UFO Digest website,
  you'll see the videographer has made a series of extraordinary
  claims about a series of differing kinds of UFOs flying over one
  night about three weeks ago. He does not seem at all objective
  in his assumptions.
  Frankly, most of the articles on UFO Digest seem rather poor,
  relatively speaking, to other UFO websites, which is really
  saying something.

  Steve

1860 Meteor Train




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 4

                                                              1860 Meteor Train
  From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  Date: Fri, 4 Jun 2010 09:48:06 +0100
  Archived: Fri, 04 Jun 2010 07:02:28 -0400
  Subject: 1860 Meteor Train

  Source: PhysOrg.Com
  http://www.physorg.com/news194798703.html
  June 3, 2010

  Astronomers Solve Walt Whitman Meteor Mystery
  On the evening of July 20, 1860, a meteor fragmented during its
  nearly-horizontal passage through the Earth's atmosphere and
  became a fireball procession, the subject of a painting by
  Frederic Church and a poem by Walt Whitman. In his landmark
  collection Leaves of Grass, famed poet Walt Whitman wrote of a
  "strange huge meteor-procession" in such vivid detail that
  scholars have debated the possible inspiration for decades.
  Now, a team of astronomers from Texas State University-San
  Marcos has applied its unique brand of forensic astronomy to the
  question, rediscovering one of the most famous celestial events
  of Whitman's day - one that inspired both Whitman and famed
  landscape painter Frederic Church - yet became inexplicably
  forgotten by modern times.
  Texas State physics professors Donald Olson and Russell
  Doescher, English professor Marilynn S. Olson and Honors Program
  student Ava G. Pope publish their findings in the July 2010
  edition of Sky & Telescope magazine, on newsstands now.
  "This is the 150th anniversary of the event that inspired both
  Whitman and Church," Donald Olson said. "It was an Earth-grazing
  meteor procession."
  Fires in the sky
  Whitman, known as a keen observer of the sky, included
  significant references to contemporary as well as cosmic events
  in his poem "Year of Meteors. (1859-60.)" published in Leaves of
  Grass. A "great comet" in the poem that appeared unexpectedly in
  the northern sky is readily identified as the Great Comet of
  1860, which follows the path Whitman described and was seen by
  most of the world.
  From Whitman's description, the Texas State research team
  immediately suspected the other celestial event he wrote about
  was the rare phenomenon known as an Earth-grazing meteor
  procession.
  "Meteor processions are so rare most people have never heard of
  them," Olson said. "There was one in 1783 and a Canadian
  fireball procession in 1913. Those were all the meteor
  processions we knew of."
  An Earth-grazing meteor is one where the trajectory takes the
  meteor through the Earth's atmosphere and back out into
  interplanetary space without ever striking the ground. A meteor
  procession occurs when a meteor breaks up upon entering the
  atmosphere, creating multiple meteors traveling in nearly
  identical paths.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m04-005.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:10]
1860 Meteor Train


  The rarity of meteor processions, however, has proven
  problematic to scholars. Whitman's description has alternately
  been ascribed to the 1833 Leonid meteor storm, the 1858 Leonids
  and a widely-observed fireball in 1859. Although Whitman is
  documented as having observed the 1833 Leonids, the Texas State
  researchers were able to discount that meteor storm because the
  timeframe conflicts with the poem's, and Whitman's descriptions
  of the two events are very different. The 1858 Leonids were also
  discounted after the research team discovered a dating error
  misattributing some of Whitman's observations of the 1833
  Leonids to the latter year.
  By contrast, the 1859 fireball was well-documented and happened
  during the timeframe of the poem. The fireball, however, was a
  single meteor, not a procession. Compounding the problem, the
  1859 fireball was a daylight meteor, whereas Whitman describes
  the procession as happening at night. The meteor procession of
  July 20, 1860, passed from west to east over a ground track of
  more than a thousand miles. The meteor descended over the Great
  Lakes and western New York, was moving horizontally when it
  reached a closest approach to the Earth's surface above the
  Hudson River valley, and then apparently escaped from the
  Earth's atmosphere over the Atlantic Ocean. (Collection of Don
  Olson)
  The art of rediscovery
  A chance clue from the 19th century artist Frederic Church
  proved key to unraveling the mystery. A decade ago, Olson saw a
  painting on the back cover of an exhibition catalog which showed
  the scene Whitman had described. Church's painting, titled "The
  Meteor of 1860," clearly depicted a meteor procession. Not only
  that, but the catalog gave the date of Church's observance: July
  20, 1860, well within the timeframe of Whitman's poem. An
  accomplished landscape painter, Church was a member of the
  Hudson River School, living beneath the same skies as Whitman.
  "We went to Church's house, and the people who know him and his
  art well, who've studied him, say, 'Oh, he wouldn't have painted
  it like that based on somebody's say-so. He must have seen it,'"
  Olson said. "The artist and his wife, who were honeymooning that
  summer, kept the painting in their bedroom for many years."
  "We went to a small research library and found old diaries of
  Theodore Cole, a friend of Church's, from July of 1860," Pope
  said. "They tell us Church was, in fact, in Catskill, New York,
  so he wasn't off in some far distant land."
  Armed with this intriguing new date, the Texas State researchers
  began poring through newspapers of the time for verification.
  What they found surprised even them. A large Earth-grazing
  meteor broke apart on the evening of July 20, 1860, creating a
  spectacular procession of multiple fireballs visible from the
  Great Lakes to New York State as it burned through the
  atmosphere and continued out over the Atlantic Ocean.
  "Any town that had a newspaper within all those states is going
  have a story on this," Olson said. "We have hundreds of
  eyewitness accounts, but there are probably hundreds more we
  don't even have.
  "From all the observations in towns up and down the Hudson River
  Valley, we're able to determine the meteor's appearance down to
  the hour and minute," Olson said. "Church observed it at 9:49
  p.m. when the meteor passed overhead, and Walt Whitman would've
  seen it at the same time, give or take one minute."
  Some of the most influential publications in the U.S. -
  including the New York Times, Smithsonian and Harper's Weekly -
  devoted major coverage to the event, and countless letters about
  it were published. Scientific American went so far as to declare
  it "the largest meteor that has ever been seen."
  "They describe it just as Church painted it. It was visible for
  about 30 seconds, and passing horizontally, so it was, in fact,
  an Earth-grazer," Pope said. "A really cool part is that the
  Catskill newspaper describes it as dividing into two parts with
  scintillations, exactly like the painting."
  This broad public attention, as well as study by many


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m04-005.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:10]
1860 Meteor Train

  professional astronomers of the day, made the meteor procession
  of 1860 one of the single most famous celestial events of its
  day, and quite possibly the most documented meteor appearance in
  history. Despite this, memory of the dazzling event faded so
  much that by the middle of the 20th century scholars were left
  puzzled over what Whitman had actually seen.
  "Its appearance, right before the Civil War, at a time growth
  and anxiety for America, made it a metaphor and portent in the
  public imagination," Marilynn Olson said.
  Provided by Texas State University

NASA Rover Finds Clue To Mars' Past And




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 4

                                          NASA Rover Finds Clue To Mars' Past And
  From: NASA News <hqnews.nul>
  Date: Thu, 03 Jun 2010 13:24:47 -0600
  Archived: Fri, 04 Jun 2010 07:05:37 -0400
  Subject: NASA Rover Finds Clue To Mars' Past And

  June 3, 2010
  Dwayne Brown
  Headquarters, Washington
  202-358-1726
  dwayne.c.brown.nul
  William Jeffs
  Johnson Space Center, Houston
  281-483-5111
  william.p.jeffs.nul
  Guy Webster
  Jet Propulsion Laboratory, Pasadena, Calif.
  818-354-6278
  guy.webster.nul
  MEDIA ADVISORY: 10-131
  NASA Rover Finds Clue To Mars' Past And Environment For Life
  PASADENA, Calif. -- Rocks examined by NASA's Spirit Mars Rover
  hold evidence of a wet, non-acidic ancient environment that may
  have been favorable for life. Confirming this mineral clue took
  four years of analysis by several scientists.
  An outcrop that Spirit examined in late 2005 revealed high
  concentrations of carbonate, which originates in wet, near-
  neutral conditions, but dissolves in acid. The ancient water
  indicated by this find was not acidic.
  NASA's rovers have found other evidence of formerly wet Martian
  environments. However the data for those environments indicate
  conditions that may have been acidic. In other cases, the
  conditions were definitely acidic, and therefore less favorable
  as habitats for life.
  Laboratory tests helped confirm the carbonate identification.
  The findings were published online Thursday, June 3 by the
  journal Science.
  "This is one of the most significant findings by the rovers,"
  said Steve Squyres of Cornell University in Ithaca, N.Y. Squyres
  is principal investigator for the Mars twin rovers, Spirit and
  Opportunity, and a co-author of the new report. "A substantial
  carbonate deposit in a Mars outcrop tells us that conditions
  that could have been quite favorable for life were present at
  one time in that place. "
  Spirit inspected rock outcrops, including one scientists called
  Comanche, along the rover's route from the top of Husband Hill
  to the vicinity of the Home Plate plateau which Spirit has
  studied since 2006. Magnesium iron carbonate makes up about one-
  fourth of the measured volume in Comanche. That is a tenfold
  higher concentration than any previously identified for
  carbonate in a Martian rock.
  "We used detective work combining results from three
  spectrometers to lock this down," said Dick Morris, lead author
  of the report and a member of a rover science team at NASA's


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m04-007.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:11]
NASA Rover Finds Clue To Mars' Past And

  Johnson Space Center in Houston."The instruments gave us
  multiple, interlocking ways of confirming the magnesium iron
  carbonate, with a good handle on how much there is."
  Massive carbonate deposits on Mars have been sought for years
  without much success. Numerous channels apparently carved by
  flows of liquid water on ancient Mars suggest the planet was
  formerly warmer, thanks to greenhouse warming from a thicker
  atmosphere than exists now. The ancient, dense Martian
  atmosphere was probably rich in carbon dioxide, because that gas
  makes up nearly all the modern, very thin atmosphere.
  It is important to determine where most of the carbon dioxide
  went. Some theorize it departed to space. Others hypothesize
  that it left the atmosphere by the mixing of carbon dioxide with
  water under conditions that led to forming carbonate minerals.
  That possibility, plus finding small amounts of carbonate in
  meteorites that originated from Mars, led to expectations in the
  1990s that carbonate would be abundant on Mars. However,
  mineral-mapping spectrometers on orbiters since then have found
  evidence of localized carbonate deposits in only one area, plus
  small amounts distributed globally in Martian dust.
  Morris suspected iron-bearing carbonate at Comanche years ago
  from inspection of the rock with Spirit's Moessbauer
  Spectrometer, which provides information about iron-containing
  minerals. Confirming evidence from other instruments emerged
  slowly. The instrument with the best capability for detecting
  carbonates, the Miniature Thermal Emission Spectrometer, had its
  mirror contaminated with dust earlier in 2005, during a wind
  event that also cleaned Spirit's solar panels.

  "It was like looking through dirty glasses," said Steve Ruff of
  Arizona State University in Tempe, Ariz., another co-author of
  the report. "We could tell there was something very different
  about Comanche compared with other outcrops we had seen, but we
  couldn't tell what it was until we developed a correction method
  to account for the dust on the mirror."
  Spirit's Alpha Particle X-ray Spectrometer instrument detected a
  high concentration of light elements, a group including carbon
  and oxygen, that helped quantify the carbonate content.
  The rovers landed on Mars in January 2004 for missions
  originally planned to last three months. Spirit has been out of
  communication since March 22 and is in a low-power hibernation
  status during Martian winter. Opportunity is making steady
  progress toward a large crater, Endeavour, which is about seven
  miles away.
  NASA's Jet Propulsion Laboratory, Pasadena, manages the Mars
  Exploration Rovers for the agency's Science Mission Directorate
  in Washington. For more information about the rovers, visit:


  http://www.nasa.gov/rovers

  -end-

  Hi, John--
  Not to be overly "caleotropic" or imbued with heated atmospheric
  gasses under the aerobic pressure of hubris or anything, I calls
  'em as I sees 'em. My "simple" explanation was merely based on
  close, direct observation. Too many of these night-scope videos
  of alleged "alien craft" are circulating on youtube and other
  sites with such a breathless and enthused interpretation and
  promotion to and from the gullible that I just I had to make
  comment about this one.
  But don't mistake me for a pelicanist - -my rule is to be
  neither a pseudo-skeptic (ala Truzzi's definition) nor a
  believer. I prefer to take a more agnostic approach. The UFO
  phenomenon, as Vallee has defined it, is real. The penultimate
  question is whether any of it involves any form of non-human
  intelligence. I suspect it may, but maintain a provisional
  attitude. Objective evidence and analysis are paramount.

  Steve

  >Subject: 1860 Meteor Train
  >Source: PhysOrg.Com
  >http://www.physorg.com/news194798703.html
  >June 3, 2010

  >Astronomers Solve Walt Whitman Meteor Mystery
  >On the evening of July 20, 1860, a meteor fragmented during its
  >nearly-horizontal passage through the Earth's atmosphere and
  >became a fireball procession, the subject of a painting by
  >Frederic Church and a poem by Walt Whitman. In his landmark
  >collection Leaves of Grass, famed poet Walt Whitman wrote of a
  >"strange huge meteor-procession" in such vivid detail that
  >scholars have debated the possible inspiration for decades.
  <snip>
  Hi Martin, Everyone,
  Another very famous painting is of a great meteor over Russia on
  February 12, 1947 that rained fireballs of molten iron on the
  Sikohote-Alin mountains of eastern Siberia. I have many fragments
  of this meteorite (a total of over 23,000 kilograms have been
  recovered) of this non-rusting "steel" object that fell from
  space in my office which I give to visitors.
  >"Meteor processions are so rare most people have never heard of
  >them," Olson said. "There was one in 1783 and a Canadian
  >fireball procession in 1913. Those were all the meteor
  >processions we knew of."
  From the recorded descriptions of 1913 meteor train by witnesses
  in Toronto (these "meteors" were mistaken for fleets of rockets
  that would periodically pass overhead in a span of nearly an
  hour - not what natural objects falling from space would do) I
  would call them UFOs, not meteors!
  We should also not forget the amazing procession of large comet
  fragments (Comet Shoemaker-Levy 9) that collided with Jupiter in
  July 1994. By coincidence, just yesterday amateur astronomers in
  Australia and the Philippines reported that they detected
  another impact on Jupiter by what they suspect was an asteroid
  or comet but there are other more interesting reasons for this
  bright flash seen on (or in front of?) the Jovian disk. Weather
  permitting, today we will look for supportive evidence using the
  York University Observatory telescopes that this was indeed a
  rare impact event.
  >An Earth-grazing meteor is one where the trajectory takes the
  >meteor through the Earth's atmosphere and back out into
  >interplanetary space without ever striking the ground. A meteor
  >procession occurs when a meteor breaks up upon entering the


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m04-015.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:15]
Re: 1860 Meteor Train

  >atmosphere, creating multiple meteors traveling in nearly
  >identical paths.
  <snip>
  There are objects known as Fast Walkers that enter the Earth's
  atmosphere from space. Most of these are natural meteoroids or
  space debris but not all - especially those that make U-turns
  and head back into space. Check out the 8mm movies on YouTube of
  the Earth-grazing daylight fireball of 1972, estimated to be the
  size of a small mountain, that entered our atmosphere over
  western U.S. and, fortunately, "skipped" back into space over
  western Canada.
  For those of you that have read this far, if you are interested
  in learning about the 4 (so far) Canadian scientists (including
  the President of the Canadain Space Agency) that walked on the
  Moon just last week and how we at York may have confirmed the
  discovery of life on Mars by examining pieces of Mars that were
  blasted off the surface of the Red Planet and made their way to
  Earth, I will be happy to share these exclusive stories in a
  follow-up post to the UFO UpDates list.

  Nick

  Arnold saw "supersonic pelicans". You are employing a technique
  used by both believers and sceptics (and politicians of all
  persuasions) to ridicule an opponent's viewpoint: find some,

  you can.
  Once upon a time, Jerry, I would have said I expected better of
  you, but unfortunately no more.

  John Rimmer

  >Sorry to be boringly pedantic, Jerry, but no-one's ever claimed
  >Arnold saw "supersonic pelicans". You are employing a technique
  >used by both believers and sceptics (and politicians of all
  >persuasions) to ridicule an opponent's viewpoint: find some,

  I think, John, that it's a question of who's disappointed whom
  longer and deeper. Sometimes I like to think that you, being an
  intelligent man, don't _really_ believe what you claim to
  believe, by which I mean your tongue is never far from cheek and
  the rest of us just aren't getting the joke. Alas, in pedestrian
  reality, you're depressingly serious.
  Unshockingly, you miss the point:
  The foolish individual who proposed pelicans as an explanation
  for Arnold's sighting was so inept that he failed to understand
  that the birds would have had to be flying at supersonic speeds.
  Thus, though this would-be debunker didn't grasp the reality, he
  was proposing supersonic pelicans by the very nature of his
  speculation.
  Hasn't this already been hashed over sufficiently for you? Or is
  it just in your nature to believe that where UFO controversies
  are concerned, there are no enemies on the far right? Does this
  make you a pelicanist or just a fellow traveler?

  Jerry Clark

                                                Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  From: Jack Brewer <brewer.jack.nul>
  Date: Sat, 5 Jun 2010 10:40:19 -0700 (PDT)
  Archived: Sat, 05 Jun 2010 16:32:49 -0400
  Subject: Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  >From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  >To: post to ufoupdates <post.nul>
  >Date: Wed, 2 Jun 2010 22:03:30 +0000
  >Subject: Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  Ms. Kasten:
  Thank you for your summary of your research into crop circles. I
  certainly agree that a percentage of the formations are the work
  of people with relatively insignificant agendas. Actually, I'm
  sure of it. The feasibility has been repeatedly and well
  established.
  That stated, I have difficulty resolving the graffiti
  explanation, for lack of a better term, with the cellular
  anomalies that have been recorded in select formations. I
  suspect you are well aware that such anomalies are interpreted
  by certain researchers such as the previously referenced Dr.
  Levengood and Vallee as indicative of microwave and/or high
  energy sources.
  To clarify, I am by no means suggesting that such apparent
  cellular deformities necessarily represent evidence of alien
  life forms. As a matter of fact, I quite seriously doubt it.
  What I am suggesting is perhaps a bit more scientific attention
  is warrented for reasons that may relate to any number of
  relevant and interesting possibilities.
  So my questions at this point to you (or anyone who would care
  to comment) are:
  Do you identify the statements of researchers such as Levengood
  and Vallee (and as the statements relate to cellular deformities
  in select crop circle formations) as credible and, if not, why
  not?
  If you are willing to accept such research as credible or
  suspend judgment on it pending further information, how do you
  resolve this with an interpretation that all crop circles are
  the results of grafitti artists?

  Thank you,
  JB

  >>evidence. In those event types listed above, sometimes there are

  >>distance but shuts off sharply at my apartment door.
  >Where can we see the evidence for this?
  I can't "show" you readings made several years ago.
  You can see the anomalous waveform illustrated
  in the booklet I referenced earlier.

  >Not very convincingly. You're not talkig about the old 'Bacon,
  >Lettuce and Tomato' claims, are you?
  Some, yes, others by different sources.
  >>And along with the EM anomalies, our group have considerable
  >>instances of sabotage, always small value to avoid a situation
  >>where police will enter the case, at home, at work, and in the
  >>automobile. They don't happen every day, but they don't stop,
  >>either. Our perpetrators love to leave pennies and cigarette
  >>butts in our homes when we're out. One target came home to find
  >>antifreeze in his dog's water dish, and the dog blind. That's
  >>our equivalent to physical trace UFO cases.
  >>So no, it's not a case of "nothing's happening."
  >So what this boils down to is "lots and lots of things are
  >happening but we just don't happen to have any solid evidence,
  >just reports of what people think is happening.
  The sabotage is solid evidence. Just as burn marks and
  impressions in the ground, and anomalies in the stalks of plants
  inside crop circles.
  >>Is that your best guest for all phenomena in all fields?
  >Yes, unless there's hard evidence, not "research by
  >proclamation" as somebody or other once said.
  How about "proclamation that there is no evidence" on the part
  of someone who simply refuses to acknowledge evidence? The
  thousands of physical trace cases, for example.
  Or radar+multiple witness sightings, or witnessed abductions.
  Years ago, I used to get upset when chronic naysayers would do
  the "no evidence" routine. But now, I realize that there will


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m05-011.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:20]
Re: Possible Links Between Crop Circles Abductions

  always be a percentage of the population which, like a record
  stuck in a groove, will declare even physical evidence as "no
  evidence," often by saying something "with a rational
  explanation" caused the physical evidence.
  Fortunately, this percentage is small in number, and not worth
  the work trying to persuade. Why expend good time and strength
  trying to persuade someone who is not ever going to change their
  viewpoint, and, who will play no part in the ultimate resolution
  of the subject at hand?
  I have found that there are people who are actually open minded,
  and I spend my time bypassing the chronic naysayers, and
  speaking and writing to the open minded. With positive results.

  Eleanor White

Arnold Revisited [was: News Links - 01-06-10]




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 6

                                        Arnold Revisited [was: News Links - 01-06-10]
  From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  Date: Sun, 6 Jun 2010 12:10:24 +0100
  Archived: Sun, 06 Jun 2010 07:15:48 -0400
  Subject: Arnold Revisited [was: News Links - 01-06-10]

  >From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>
  >Date: Sat, 5 Jun 2010 10:13:20 -0500
  >Subject: Re: News Links - 01-06-10
  >>From: John Rimmer <johnrimmer.nul>
  >>To: post.nul
  >>Date: Sat, 5 Jun 2010 14:50:39 +0000 (GMT)
  >>Subject: Re: News Links - 01-06-10
  >>>From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  >>>To: <post.nul>
  >>>Date: Fri, 4 Jun 2010 15:16:05 -0500
  >>>Subject: Re: News Links - 01-06-10
  >>>Not really that difficult to define. It's a word I invented to
  >>>characterize one who conjures up absurd explanations which
  >>>pretend to be conventional, as did the goofball who,
  >>>irrespective of physical laws and basic good sense, proposed
  >>>that Ken Arnold saw supersonic pelicans.
  >>Sorry to be boringly pedantic, Jerry, but no-one's ever claimed
  >>Arnold saw "supersonic pelicans". You are employing a technique
  >>used by both believers and sceptics (and politicians of all
  >>persuasions) to ridicule an opponent's viewpoint: find some,
  >>misleading, trivial slogan and push it as far and as often as
  >>you can.
  >>Once upon a time, Jerry, I would have said I expected better of
  >>you, but unfortunately no more.
  >I think, John, that it's a question of who's disappointed whom
  >longer and deeper. Sometimes I like to think that you, being an
  >intelligent man, don't _really_ believe what you claim to
  >believe, by which I mean your tongue is never far from cheek and
  >the rest of us just aren't getting the joke. Alas, in pedestrian
  >reality, you're depressingly serious.
  >Unshockingly, you miss the point:
  >The foolish individual who proposed pelicans as an explanation
  >for Arnold's sighting was so inept that he failed to understand
  >that the birds would have had to be flying at supersonic speeds.
  >Thus, though this would-be debunker didn't grasp the reality, he
  >was proposing supersonic pelicans by the very nature of his
  >speculation.
  >Hasn't this already been hashed over sufficiently for you? Or is
  >it just in your nature to believe that where UFO controversies
  >are concerned, there are no enemies on the far right? Does this
  >make you a pelicanist or just a fellow traveler?
  Hi Jerry & John
  As I've been spending quite a lot of time lately on revisiting
  the Arnold case in detail, including the pelican controversy,
  can I offer the prospect of some middle ground? I found that it
  is not actually geometrically impossible to reconstruct things
  so that Arnold could have seen subsonic pelicans transiting


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m06-005.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:23]
Arnold Revisited [was: News Links - 01-06-10]

  ~80deg of bearing angle in 102 sec as reported.
  But - and it's a big but - this requires Arnold to have turned
  left on a northeasterly course, which is highly unlikely on
  various practical grounds, inconsistent with numerous pieces of
  latent circumstantial evidence, and explicitly contradicted in a
  June 26 1947 quotation attributed to Arnold. So in terms of
  angular speed alone John can have his pelicans, although at a
  cost which needs to be reckoned up and paid first.
  But then other problems with the hypothesis emerge and serve to
  multiply that initial down-payment, including:
  - that the angular size of pelicans at the maximum range
  consistent with the angular rate would have been so large that
  they should have been plainly identifiable;
  - that the range from Arnold to the pelicans would have been
  decreasing throughout, not increasing, so that the angular size
  would increase instead of dwindling below resolution as Arnold
  observed, the birds becoming, larger, brighter and clearer by at
  least a factor 2 as they approached Mt Adams;
  - that the claimed match between the objects' flight
  characteristics and typical pelican flight characteristics
  proves weak when examined quantitatively against measurements of
  the latter in the literature;
  - and that the original justification for the nearby-bird
  hypothesis was Kottmeyer's claim that the principal distance cue
  reported by Arnold - occultation behind the "jagged peak" on Mt
  Rainier - could not be reliable since (he said) no such peak
  seemed to exist. Easton then presented evidence that
  "established" Kottmeyer's claim, and argued that his own
  positive identification of the jagged peak as Little Tacoma, on
  the eastern side of the mountain, was proof that the observed
  occultation must have been illusory. But this argument is
  illusory: Kottmeyer was wrong. Easton's assumed sightline is
  more than 90=BA in error; Little Tacoma was not visible from
  anywhere on Arnold's flight path; and there is indeed a suitable
  "jagged peak" just where Arnold said it was.
  There is more. The pelican theory is superficially attractive
  and I understand why it has been so influential. But in short
  the theory doesn't work without assuming really very large
  errors in, for example, Arnold's clock reading which, when you
  actually try it out, are very hard to justify and don't make
  sense. So I may have been misleading at the beginning. I don't
  think the middle ground is really quite in the middle. It's
  significantly nearer to Jerry than to John.

  Martin Shough

Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

                                                Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Mon, 07 Jun 2010 11:01:19 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 08 Jun 2010 06:32:33 -0400
  Subject: Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  >From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  >To: post to ufoupdates <post.nul>
  >Date: Sun, 6 Jun 2010 18:28:39 +0000
  >Subject: Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  >And, yes, I can say that graffiti is exactly the term that
  >applies to the formations.
  >And, just like art, it is all in the eyes of the beholder. Isn't
  >it.
  If you think of it as only graffiti, then that's all you will
  see. Some of these formations contain information only
  discovered by looking hard for it. This means considering the
  possibility that they are more than just doodles transferred
  from a paper drawing to a field.
  An excellent example of what I mean is the recent May 22, 2010,
  Wilton Windmill formation. It has a pleasing appearance which
  you might enjoy. But people soon recognized that some of the
  marks could be decoded to represent ASCII characters. The first
  discovery was that some of these symbols represented Euler's
  formula, as was mentioned earlier on the list. People all over
  said, "Wow, isn't that amazing!".
  But that was only the beginning for me. A different, but non-
  independent, subset of bits was found by an anonymous person to
  contain another sequence of printable characters that did not
  seem to mean anything. I discovered that it was a high-level
  algorithm for decoding an ASCII string to a grayscale image. I
  implemented the algorithm and was able to decode a meaningful
  image from the 2002 Crabwood formation. If you recall, this was
  the one with the "alien" face next to a disc shape containing
  over 1300 bits in a spiral. It was decoded back then to a string
  of text. The 2010 algorithm let me decode those same bits in the
  2002 formation into a 9x9 image array. After Gaussian smoothing,
  it seemed to show a bipedal figure extending a three-fingered
  hand in greeting. For whatever reason, the algorithm allowing us
  to see this image was not given until about eight years later.
  http://www.treurniet.ca/CropForms/CrabWilton.htm
  And it just gets better. I wondered whether the Wilton Windmill
  formation itself could contain sufficient information to
  represent an image. With the help of Zef Damen's reconstruction,
  one can see that there are 288 bits in the formation that might
  be used for this purpose. I discovered that I could read these
  bits in three different ways to get three meaningful and
  completely different 9x4 images.
  http://www.treurniet.ca/CropForms/WiltonPic.htm
  Each image was generated by starting to read the bits at the same
  point in the formation where Euler's formula was found to begin. To
  counter the argument that the resulting images are merely random
  pixels, I generated another 7 images for each case by offsetting the
  starting point for each scheme by 1-7 bits. In my opinion, the 21
  images with the offsets make much less sense compared to the three
  images with zero offset. See if you agree.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m08-003.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:26]
Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past


  http://www.treurniet.ca/CropForms/OffsetComparisons.htm
  The image content in each case seems trivial. I interpreted them
  to be 1) a bust of a bunny with long ears pointing up, 2) a
  humanoid figure waving hello, and 3) a child riding a tricycle.
  I suggest that, if there is a message, it is the very
  innocuousness of the images.
  What I find most amazing about this formation is that two text
  strings and three meaningful images can be decoded from the same
  spatial array of bits, merely by choosing different schemes for
  reading them. In the Crabwood formation, as well, the same bits
  encoded the text and an image. I suggest that this is not an
  easy thing to design. At the least, it would require computing
  power and a clever optimization algorithm to achieve. This is
  humanly possible, probably, but it places the credit for these
  formations in high places having the necessary resources. Of
  course, we already knew this, judging from the technology
  required to produce the physical effects people have found.
  So formations such as this are only graffiti to some people, but
  they can be a source of additional amazement if you care to
  investigate further.

  William

   the third breach of classified data to the public since the

  Hi Eleanor, Stanton, Everyone!
  Late last month York University held a special 2 day conference
  in honour of distinguished Canadian space scientist Gordon
  Shepherd who earlier this year stepped down as Chairman of our
  Centre For Research in Earth and Space Science. My ex-physics
  classmate from York, astronaut Steve MacLean (now President of
  the Canadian Space Agency) was among the many top space
  scientists and engineers that participated at this conference.
  One international space project that Canada has been invited to
  be part of is a Sample Return Mission to Mars that is intended
  to provide answers to our questions of past or present life on
  Mars. It is estimated that this robotic mission to Mars will
  cost upwards of 10 billion dollars! This very expensive mission
  is, in my opinion, scientifically unverifiable (unless we plan
  further sample return missions to confirm our findings) and even
  totally unnecessary since we have samples that were blasted off
  the surface of Mars that are already here on Earth!
  Of the hundreds of meteorites and many other extraterrestrial
  artifacts that we have studied here and are currently in my
  possession, over a dozen of these have been identified by
  planetary scientists as having originated on Mars and other
  Solar System bodies including the Moon, possibly Mercury as well
  as a few large asteroids such as Vesta.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m08-005.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:27]
Re: Walking On The Moon


  Earlier this year I did what my colleagues thought was not worth
  doing - searching for microfossils and other evidence of life
  inside our Martian rock fragements that we broke apart (not cut
  and polish as others do) and examined under a powerful Scanning
  Electron Microscope (SEM) which also had the ability of doing
  elemental analysis and a chemical comparison between the
  suspected microfossils embedded in the Martian rock with the
  surrounding minerals. To our surprise we discovered things that
  seemingly confirm many previous findings of life on Mars (eg.
  20th century spectroscopy absorption studies of the seasonal
  albedo variations on Mars that were best interpreted as plant
  life; the 1976 Viking lander Labeled Release experiments that
  were designed to search for present life on Mars; the unexpected
  announcement in 1996 by President Clinton that microfossils were
  discovered in a Martian meteorite recovered in Antarctica;
  etc.).
  Our initial findings of what appears to be microfossils inside
  Mars meteorite North West Africa 998 were shared with experts
  here, including biologists and geologists who have previously
  identified what they believe to be the first living organisms on
  our planet in rocks that they have determined to be 3 billion
  years old or more. To these experts our suspected microfossils
  in NWA 998 are very similar to the microfossils found in these
  very old rocks from Earth. Until further tests are done, I have
  resisted publishing our initial amazing findings but would like
  to share them with my colleagues and the UFO UpDates list.
  As for those first four Canadians to have walked on the Moon
  last month since 12 Apollo astronauts did so back in the late
  1960s and early 1970s, this was achieved by doing what, to my
  knowledge, none of my colleagues have done before - ask visitors
  to remove their shoes and actually walk on my Mars and Moon
  rocks! To date there are nearly 500 staff and faculty members,
  researchers, students, friends of York University, etc. to have
  walked on Mars (and possibly trampled on Martians too!) but
  still only 4 who can claim to have walked on the Moon - all with
  certificates to prove it! The Moon rock these 4 have walked on
  is believed to be from the lunar highlands and is geologically
  identical to the famous 'Genesis Rock' that was brought back to
  Earth by fellow Apollo 15 Moon walkers Dave Scott and Jim Irwin.
  Jim Irwin later got involved in the search for Noah's Ark. Jim's
  talk at York University in the 1970s inspired me to continue
  this search myself - with positive results I may add - but this
  is a different story of my adventures, this time in eastern
  Turkey near the borders of Armenia, Iran and Iraq. It confirmed
  to me that a global catastrophy in earliest historic times as
  described in the Bible (and over 100 other written and oral
  traditions by peoples and cultures around the world) which
  involved ET intervention was historical truth rather than
  mythology...

  Nick Balaskas

                                                         Secrecy News -- 06/07/10
  From: Steven Aftergood <saftergood.nul>
  Date: Mon, 07 Jun 2010 11:48:38 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 08 Jun 2010 06:42:04 -0400
  Subject: Secrecy News -- 06/07/10

  SECRECY NEWS
  from the FAS Project on Government Secrecy
  Volume 2010, Issue No. 47
  June 7, 2010
  Secrecy News Blog: http://www.fas.org/blog/secrecy/

  **      UN PROPOSES "GOOD PRACTICES" FOR INTELLIGENCE
  **      ANOTHER LEAK ARREST
  **      OSC VIEWS LEFT-WING CRIME IN GERMANY

  UN PROPOSES "GOOD PRACTICES" FOR INTELLIGENCE
  The United Nations Human Rights Council last week presented a new set of
  institutional and policy practices for intelligence agencies that it said
  would help to improve accountability and protection of human rights in
  intelligence policy.
  The new "Compilation of good practices on legal and institutional
  frameworks and measures to ensure respect for human rights by intelligence
  agencies while countering terrorism" discusses 35 principles and practices
  in four categories: legal mandate, oversight, compliance with human rights
  standards, and issues related to specific intelligence functions. The
  compilation was prepared for the UN by Special Rapporteur Martin Scheinin
  with the assistance of the Geneva Centre for the Democratic Control of
  Armed Forces.
  http://www.fas.org/irp/eprint/unhrc.pdf
  The UN Human Rights Council does not have (or claim to have) authority to
  dictate the intelligence policies of member nations. Moreover, the
  proposed "good practices" do not distinguish between democracies and
  dictatorships, or among governments that have an independent judiciary and
  those that came to power through electoral fraud or military coup. The
  practices also do not differentiate among domestic, foreign and military
  intelligence services, though each of these may be subject to different
  legal and policy frameworks. "It is not the purpose of this compilation
  to promulgate a set of normative standards that should apply at all times
  and in all parts of the world," the document states.
  Indeed, "Very few States have included all of the practices outlined below
  in their legal and institutional frameworks for intelligence services and
  their oversight. Some States will be able to identify themselves as
  following the majority of the 35 elements of good practice. Other States
  may start by committing themselves to a small number of these
  elements...."
  But Mr. Scheinin told the Council last week that he "hoped that States
  would use his compilation of good practices in an assessment of their own
  law and practice, and identify the areas of full adherence, of partial
  adherence and of non-adherence. Thereafter, they would hopefully determine
  the areas where they wished to adhere with the identified good practices in
  the future and set benchmarks for getting there." In other words, the
  compilation can serve as a template which citizens can use for comparing
  and evaluating national intelligence policies.
  In many respects, the United States is among the nations with the best


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m08-006.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:27]
Secrecy News -- 06/07/10

  intelligence practices, with a relatively well-developed legal framework
  for intelligence activities and a mature oversight apparatus. But in
  other respects, it falls short.
  For example, the UN compilation's Practice 9 requires that "Any individual
  who believes that her or his rights have been infringed by an intelligence
  service [should be] able to bring a complaint to a court or oversight
  institution.... Individuals affected by the illegal actions of an
  intelligence service [should] have recourse to an institution that can
  provide an effective remedy, including full reparation for the harm
  suffered."
  But in cases like those of Khaled el-Masri and Maher Arar -- who appear to
  have been wrongly detained, "rendered" abroad, and tortured -- no
  adjudication or remedy is available from the U.S. government since their
  complaints have been deflected by the use of the state secrets privilege.
  More generally, intelligence oversight in the U.S. has failed to generate
  a consensual public record concerning the extraordinary intelligence
  activities of the post-9/11 era, leaving the field open to continuing
  allegations of abuse and violations of law. Today, Physicians for Human
  Rights issued a report alleging that CIA medical personnel were complicit
  in unlawful human experimentation through their collaboration in
  monitoring the application of "enhanced" interrogation techniques.
  (http://phrtorturepapers.org). The CIA denies the charge.
  In another instance where U.S. practice falls short of the ideal, the UN
  compilation's Practice 3 directs that "The powers and competences of
  intelligence services [should be] clearly and exhaustively defined in
  national law."
  But the terms of current U.S. intelligence law are not entirely clear or
  exhaustive. We don't know, for example, the current nature or scope of
  domestic surveillance activities or what exactly was permitted under the
  most recently enacted amendments to the Foreign Intelligence Surveillance
  Act.
  That absence of public clarity is deliberate, said NSA Director Gen. Keith
  B. Alexander, the new Commander of US Cyber Command, in a speech at the
  Center for Strategic and International Studies last week.
  http://csis.org/files/attachments/100603_alexander_transcript.pdf
  In response to a question from Kate Martin of the Center for National
  Security Studies about the feasibility of increasing public disclosure of
  cyber security policy, Gen. Alexander said that that was not his preferred
  approach. Instead, he promised, government officials would be fully candid
  with each other, while continuing to withhold information from the public.
  In other words, he said, what Americans can expect is "transparency at the
  classified level."

  ANOTHER LEAK ARREST
  The U.S. Army has arrested Spc. Bradley Manning of Potomac, Maryland for
  unauthorized disclosure of classified information. Among other things, he
  is suspected of having provided the video of a 2007 Apache helicopter
  strike in Baghdad that killed several civilians to the Wikileaks web site,
  which published it online in April of this year. The story was reported
  last night by Wired's Threat Level blog. See "U.S. Intelligence Analyst
  Arrested in Wikileaks Video Probe" by Kevin Poulsen and Kim Zetter:
  http://www.wired.com/threatlevel/2010/06/leak/
  Spc. Manning is currently being held in pre-trial confinement in Kuwait,
  according to an Army statement obtained by NPR.
  His arrest is the third known apprehension of a suspected leaker during
  the Obama Administration, after Shamai Leibowitz and Thomas A. Drake, and
  seems to reflect an increasingly aggressive response to unauthorized
  disclosures of classified information.

  OSC VIEWS LEFT-WING CRIME IN GERMANY
  "German security authorities reported a substantial increase in crime and
  attacks on police in 2009 related to left-wing political groups and
  individuals," said a new report from the DNI Open Source Center.
  "According to Germany's Interior Ministry, more extremist crimes and acts
  of violence occurred in 2009 than in any year since 2001. The ministry
  reported that in 2009, left-wing extremist crimes increased by almost 40%


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m08-006.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:27]
Secrecy News -- 06/07/10

  to 9,375," the report said.
  "The Berlin intelligence service chief called his city the 'German
  stronghold of left-wing extremism,' noting 2,200 resident radical
  individuals, 950 of whom are 'autonomous' leftist anarchists. According to
  the police, the number of leftist crimes in Berlin doubled to 1,300 in
  2009.... This increase in left-wing crime represents an additional concern
  alongside Germany's perceived problems with right-wing extremist and
  immigrant crime....Federal and local government officials have initiated
  measures to combat left-wing extremism," the report said.
  A copy of the unclassified report, marked For Official Use Only," was
  obtained by Secrecy News. See "German Left-Wing Crime Increase Adds to
  Public Security Concerns," Open Source Center, April 27, 2010.
  http://www.fas.org/irp/world/germany/crime.pdf

  The people who created this PIA10214 photo, with all those other

  PIA10214 photo where it is obvious that if someone would like to
  see an object as it really is that person must move one of those
  snapshots along that line and that is what I did. It takes a lot
  of time to do so but I can recommend others to work that way.

  From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>

  Subject: Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  >Date: Mon, 07 Jun 2010 11:01:19 -0400

  <snip>
  >An excellent example of what I mean is the recent May 22, 2010,
  >Wilton Windmill formation. It has a pleasing appearance which
  >you might enjoy. But people soon recognized that some of the
  >marks could be decoded to represent ASCII characters. The first
  >discovery was that some of these symbols represented Euler's
  >formula, as was mentioned earlier on the list. People all over
  >said, "Wow, isn't that amazing!".
  <snip>
  I am not generally interested in crop markings any more, but I
  couldn't let this pass without comment. The fact that ASCII code
  was incorporated in the design is an extremely strong argument
  for a human origin of the marking.
  In order to be able to generate meaningful ASCII messages, an
  intelligence would need to be familiar with the ASCII code. That
  implies a knowledge of the alphabet, and whatever language the
  'message' was written in.
  With the knowledge of the alphabet and the language, why would
  an alien intelligence wanting to communicate do so in such a way
  that the message might not be immediately recognised?
  Conclusion: it was a human-generated puzzle.

  Joe

  I explicitly said "this is humanly possible" in the the part you
  snipped. It is no less marvelous that someone, especially a
  human, could encode all this orthogonal information in the same
  set of spatially organized bits. I salute the human that did
  this, if that's who it was.
  I would add that it was certainly not people like Doug and Dave
  with their planks that did this.
  >In order to be able to generate meaningful ASCII messages, an
  >intelligence would need to be familiar with the ASCII code. That
  >implies a knowledge of the alphabet, and whatever language the

  Your conclusion that it was a human-generated puzzle is not a
  necessary one. Critics often say, why doesn't ET talk to us
  instead of placing these obscure glyphs in the fields? Then,
  when he does communicate using our language, people say that
  proves he must be human.
  You wonder, if it is ET, why write messages that are not
  immediately recognized? Perhaps ET is not used to communicating
  with words, being telepathic an' all. Using visual symbols may
  be more natural for them. Or maybe they are playful and like to
  present us with puzzles. I know I enjoyed working it out. Or
  maybe they think we won't be as frightened by them if we have to


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m08-009.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:30]
Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  work at gradually learning about them. I could guess at a number
  of other possible reasons why. So our not recognizing the
  message immediately, if there is one, does not logically require
  that the author be human.
  The military-industrial complex may well have covert
  electromagnetic technologies capable of laying down crops in the
  field. There is where I would start looking for human crop
  formation makers. I'm also open to the possibility that ET is
  responsible. In either case, we can only guess at motive.

  William

  >responsible. In either case, we can only guess at motive.
  No they don't. Why would they even waste the money when a few
  boards and a tape measure will do the job for you. Always with
  the cop-out 'secret military technology or vehicle'. As for the
  motive; great snicker value at your expense, William. Just
  another form of graffitti.
  Once upon a time there was some value - perhaps still - to trace
  evidence in a field or a clearing in the forest after someone
  reported seeing one or more UAP.
  Investigators might find a small circular swale in the grass or
  crop while oft times - and perhaps more interestingly - a
  crushed down area, usually circular in nature, would be found in
  the forest.
  Everything up to and including 6 inch thick, 25 foot high,
  spruce trees would be found crushed down to a height of two
  feet; and in one case in my own area an 18 inch wide band was
  seared into the vegetation at the outer perimeter. Now to me;
  that is interesting.
  One can imagine the weight required to crush down a dozen or
  more 6 inch thick spruce trees and the heat generated to sear a
  band around the perimeter.
  Crop circles bore me to tears.

  Don Ledger

   that in any digital modification software.

   devices can't detect it. However, if we modify our equipment to
   various bands we might.

   effects and see what can be done.

  Yes, and apparently quite thoroughly, I might add. One of the
  reasons that I am interested in your perspectives is due to my
  interpretation that you are not star struck, so to speak, by the
  inherent stumbling blocks that frequently de-rail objectivity
  when addressing this type of subject matter.
  >I didn't know that Vallee has commented on crop circles. Please
  >cite the documentation you are quoting from.
  Sure. Among other sources, in an article titled, Crop Circles
  Part Deux: Alien Glyphs, Human Myths, Blogging Bliss, author Dr.
  Vallee wrote:
  "Given that _some_ of the patterns were obviously man-made
  hoaxes, it was possible to compare the effect on the plants in
  genuine versus bogus patterns. Under the microscope the results
  were clear: if you push a board across a wheat field to flatten
  it, you will break the stalks between nodes because the nodes
  are thicker and stronger. But in the unexplained, complex
  patterns the nodes themselves were exploded, often keeping the
  fibers intact. Conclusion: something was coupling energy into
  the plants in the form of heat... Therefore the idea of a beam
  weapon is indeed one of the scenarios to consider."
  Full article:
  http://www.boingboing.net/2010/04/08/crop-circles-part-de.html#more
  Thanks again, KK. I appreciate and respect your level-headed and
  objective approach.

  JB

UFOs & Conventions




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 8

                                                            UFOs & Conventions
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Tue, 8 Jun 2010 16:28:26 EDT
  Archived: Tue, 08 Jun 2010 16:59:25 -0400
  Subject: UFOs & Conventions

  Hi Listers,
  A few weeks ago I mentioned some folks who were putting on a
  comic book/sci-fi convention and I had asked Richard C. Hoagland
  and George Noory if they wanted to be guests. Mainly because
  Richard and George's producer Tom had asked me about comic
  conventions from time to time.
  Well the convention took place, not as huge as expected and the
  promoters of the event need to do some studying to get their
  acts together but something did happen that was extremely
  delightful.
  George Noory did a small panel along with writer Marc Zicree and
  one of the news directors for Los Angeles radio station KFI.
  It was a fun panel where they talked a lot about the Twilight
  Zone series. That was fun enough for us in the audience, but
  what had us all practically on the floor in stitches was George
  Noory's tales of hosting Coast To Coast AM and the full spectrum
  of subjects he covers.
  People were literally holding onto their chairs and one another
  to keep from falling down and rolling on the floor!
  George Noory is a kind, professional gentleman and he had the
  audience in stitches! I thought someone was going to hurt
  themselves each time he recounted a funny incident about UFOs,
  bigfoot, ghosts, etc. Even his lovely daughter he brought along
  with him was giggling at his stories.
  I sat there thinking what would have happened if he had Alfred
  Lehmberg or Dick Hoagland or any number of you folks on this
  List with a good sense of humor. You would have had to carry
  people out of there with oxygen tanks.
  I mean it was so funny I wanted to bolt out of the room so I
  wouldn't embarrass myself laughing. It so showed me the public
  needs to laugh when facing scary things. The need to deliver the
  message in a cordial and humorous message without being negative
  and harsh towards those who experience these paranormal events.
  In other words something I asked the late Dr. John Mack about
  regarding the type of bedside manner one should take regarding
  abductees and experiencers. Dr. Mack put us straight right then
  and there about the importance of just that and I have it on
  video.
  After George's panel discussion the room literally brightened up
  and people crowded him as he tried to leave the auditorium.
  Folks who were shy to speak suddenly opened up and that's
  important because every bit of clear minded data we can get
  helps all of us doing research.
  I'm hoping to work on doing more public events on my own and I
  can see a great value in having ufologists teamed up with celebs
  in various fields as this promotes an across the board mix that
  not only educates but entertains the public.

                                             Secret Empire; Eisenhower, The CIA, And...
  From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  Date: Tue, 8 Jun 2010 23:11:26 +0000
  Archived: Wed, 09 Jun 2010 06:59:15 -0400
  Subject: Secret Empire; Eisenhower, The CIA, And...

  Secret Empire; Eisenhower, The CIA, And The Hidden Story Of
  America's Space Espionage
  by Philip Taubman, page 139,
  This Thing is Made Out of Toilet Paper

  [Start Citation]
  The air of mystery surrounding the nuclear test site, combined
  with unidentified flying objects that were occasionally seen
  high in the skies around the site, made the government
  reservation the subject of intense speculation.
  Over the years. Area 51, which encompassed the airfield as well
  as the surrounding nuclear test site, became famously associated
  with reports about alien spaceships.
  Most of the UFO sightings were stimulated by the new Lockheed
  plane and the subsequent test flights of ever-more-advanced
  aircraft that the government secretly developed during the cold
  war. Many of the sightings came at sunset when the lower
  atmosphere was already cloaked in darkness but the sun could
  still illuminate a plane flying in the stratosphere.
  In an era when no one knew the United States was building planes
  that could fly that high, the sight of the sunlight reflecting
  off an object streaking across the edge of space led to dozens
  of alarmed reports, many of them from commercial airline pilots
  mystified by the view as they flew across the Southwest.
  [End Citation]

  Make what you will of the above statement. As usual, I consider
  it prudent to hedge one's bets. However, whatever material I
  read I discover that no one has figured out what is going on
  with truly unidentified craft.

  KK

  Hi Jack, Kathy & List,
  Regarding Vallee's blog posts for boingboing on crop circles,
  see also:
  http://bit.ly/dzFGRl
  and
  http://bit.ly/9MxWzi
  so far, Vallee has made three posts for boingboing on the
  subject of crop circles, the two I cited here, and the one Jack
  noted for Kathy's benefit. There is supposed to be a fourth,
  summary post by Vallee on crop circles, but it has not appeared
  on boingboing as yet.
  Interestingly, Vallee also cites via link some of the BLT crop
  circle research, which I find somewhat dubious in empirical
  evidence and objectivity. Also, if you read the comments
  following each of these three posts by Vallee, you'll note an
  interesting objection to the idea that Vallee first posed almost
  20 years ago in regard to how some crop circles might have been
  created by use of microwaves or from an airborne platform using
  maser technology (microwave laser) - if this were so, that an


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m09-003.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:35]
Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  EMR source from an aerial man-made platform, such as a
  helicopter or blimp were employed, how come the vegetation in
  crop circles is bent down from just above the ground?
  Wouldn't such concentrated microwave radiation directed from
  above affect the crop from the top down, and cause a wilting or
  bending effect _throughout_ the plant stalks or fibers, from the
  top to bottom, rather than just at the bottom or just above the
  ground?
  I suppose some kind of "ground effect" or "bounce" might be
  involved in the use of such proposed technology, which some-
  how couples energy at or near the ground plane, where the
  "exploded nodes" of the various kinds of vegetation crop circles
  have been created in are affected or bent at a lower level, but
  this seems rather dubious also, in lieu of an explanation of the
  mechanism of such a "ground effect" that leaves the upper part
  of the plants unaffected. Vallee has yet to respond to these
  queries of why, if such airborne-originated radiation might be
  being used, it only allegedly affects the plants in a manner
  where the lowest portion of the plant stalks are bent over
  or have "exploded nodes."
  While I respect and admire Vallee for his visionary explorations
  and investigation of the ufo phenomenon, I find his recent posts
  on crop circles rather too speculative and insufficiently
  explained, from a technical and physics perspective, to be very
  credible or likely. Perhaps he'll cover these objections in part
  four of his boingboing posts, whenever that appears online.
  Otherwise, such posts of his on crop circles will remain only
  speculative, and, shall I say, "ungrounded." 8^}

  Steve

  of inevitable disclosure of what is really going on "out there",
  how would they go about preparing for it? I'm coming to the view
  that there may be a balancing act going on to minimize the
  expected pain. The purpose is to bring about the required change
  in world view gradually to lessen the impact.
  Along with the "real stuff", like anomalous crop formations and
  the ET's flying saucers, the PTB has figured out how to create
  these phenomena, or at least, make people think they can. If
  people have an inkling that some other members of their own
  species are creating these effects, they will take that as an
  explanation if they can't accept the "real stuff".
  Care is taken to always create "plausible deniability" so that
  people are not forced to change their world view. As the
  thinking may go, although people can't be prevented from
  exposure to a greater reality, they don't have to accept it if
  they are unable, when conventional explanations are available.
  This strategy is helped along by the entertainment industry and
  by the behavior of the media after sightings occur. The
  intention is to foster and control a change in world view
  without forcing it to happen.
  Some proportion of crop formations are clearly man-made using
  low-tech methods. The rest are made using more advanced
  technologies, and these are controlled either by humans or by
  someone else. With respect to UFOs, some are the "real stuff",
  while others are covert technologies that many people think they
  should not understand for national security reasons. In both
  cases, the existing belief system is an option when confronted
  with ET intelligence.
  I think this modus operandi will continue until something
  happens that will make it too obvious what is really going on.
  According to this strategy, there need not be any public
  announcement by the PTB (so-called disclosure) until that
  happens.
  So you and others are fitting quite nicely into this scheme. You
  are merely accepting the status quo being offered to you (boards
  and tape measure method), instead of modifying your world view
  based on the evidence.
  The strategy has been so successful that it's not hard to
  believe that the UFO evidence and crop formation technology has
  all come from covert technology developments. For those of us
  without direct experience with ETs, the most persuasive evidence
  for their existence may be the existence of this disinformation
  program itself. It's very reason for being is fear of the
  societal effect of a drastic change in world view forced by
  sudden indisputable evidence of ET intelligence.
  A logical argument about the existence of ETs (i.e., non-human
  intelligence) might go as follows:
  (1) We know there are advanced technologies involved in making
  crop formations.
  (2) If controlled by humans, the likely purpose is to camouflage
  ET involvement. What other non-trivial reason could there be?
  (3) If not controlled by humans, then ETs are in control.
  (4) So, in either case, there are ETs.

  William

  Then let me try again. I'll do my best to keep it simple.
  Both Haselhoff and Levengood independently found that plant
  nodes in crop circles change in length as a function of distance
  from the center of the circle. The correlation becomes almost
  perfect using a model of an EM point source hanging a few meters
  above the center of the circle. This means that it is highly
  likely that an EM point source generated heat to change the node
  lengths of the plants. See the following for a longer summary.
  http://www.swirlednews.com/article.asp?artID=185
  This correlation does not happen by flattening a crop with a
  board.
  >As you learn, William, you will find that the marvels are in the
  >small but inexplicable details, not in the flambouyant such as
  >crop circles and explainable spirals in a Norwegian sky.
  Then Haselhoff's finding is a fine example of a marvelous,
  inexplicable detail that you should appreciate.
  >Some CCs are good artwork in the style of graffitti but they
  >aren't proved as being associatied with the phenomenon that
  >interests me and which is the focus of this list.
  If by that you mean UFOs, then these have often been associated
  with crop circles. These balls of light may not fly over
  airports or violate aviation safety rules, but they are still
  unidentified flying objects. It might even be the case that the
  study of crop formations could shed some light on the nature of
  UFOs.

  I respect your experience as far as it goes, Don, but it does

  circles.
  Anyone who says it's all just grafitti executed with ropes and

  that way, but many are not. And, even if the latter are also
  just grafitti, no one knows what tools the artist is using. That
  an unknown technology is suspected is reason enough to
  investigate the subject further.

  given when you concentrate the Sun's energy in a flattened area
  rather than the dispersed and less compacted grasses outside of
  the circle. People have reported getting light headed while in
  crop circles. Sounds more like a religious experience than some
  physical effect.
  I know there has been a effort to connect crop circles with the
  UFO phenomenon but the fact is this is an outgrowth of the trace
  cases that were recorded some years ago. These came to light in
  the media; just simple swales and bedded down grass in the area
  where some appreciably sized object was seen to have rested.
  Once it became public then more of these popped up as discovered
  by farmers or woodsmen and even pilots flying the local area.
  Then one day rather than depressions these stylized circles
  began showing up.
  They got publicity then a couple of guys claimed they had made
  them at night, then more people got into the act. People had
  been duped but that didn't stop those really intrigued by the
  concept. Once interest started to flag we saw that balls of
  light were appearing around crop circles and were suspected of
  creating these classic, grassey graphics. It has come full
  circle.
  Sorry William, I'm not convinced.

  Don Ledger
