Butterfly Wings And UFOs




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 11

                                                       Butterfly Wings And UFOs
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Thu, 10 Jun 2010 16:16:01 -0400
  Archived: Fri, 11 Jun 2010 06:09:28 -0400
  Subject: Butterfly Wings And UFOs

  I just ran across this interesting article on how the color of a
  butterfly wing is related to the micro-structure of its surface.
  http://www.world-science.net/othernews/100531_butterfly
  The story reminds me of photographs containing an image of a UFO
  when nothing was seen by the naked eye at the time the picture
  was taken. The object in the photo is usually explained as an
  insect or a bird near the camera, but the butterfly wing offers
  another explanation.
  To quote from the article...
  -----
  Researchers at the University of Cambridge studied the
  Indonesian Peacock or Swallowtail butterfly, or Papilio blumei,
  whose wing scales consist of intricate, microscopic structures
  that resemble the inside of an egg carton. These structures
  produce intense colors because of their shape and the fact that
  they consist of alternate layers of air and cuticle, a hard
  material.
  Using a combination of procedures for fabrication at minute
  scales, or nanofabrication, the researchers reported that they
  made structurally identical copies of the butterfly scales that
  produced the same vivid colors as the wings.
  Intriguingly, the butterfly may also be using its colors to
  encrypt itself appearing one color to potential mates but
  another color to predators ... “The shiny green patches on this
  tropical butterfly’s wing scales are a stunning example of
  nature’s ingenuity in optical design. Seen with the right
  optical equipment these patches appear bright blue, but with the
  naked eye they appear green.
  This could explain why the butterfly has evolved this way of
  producing color. If its eyes see fellow butterflies as bright
  blue, while predators only see green patches in a green tropical
  environment, then it can hide from predators at the same time as
  remaining visible to members of its own species".
  -----
  If the surface of the UFO has a micro-structure like the
  butterfly wings, changes in UFO visiblity might happen too. The
  camera optics might see it at one wavelength, while the eye
  receives it at a different wavelength that might be either the
  same as the background or not even in the visible spectrum.
  Also, if the micro-structure could be varied dynamically over
  its surface by an operator, that might help to explain how some
  UFOs appear to morph from one shape to another. Different parts
  might change visibility to the naked eye over time.
  We also hear occasionally of UFOs appearing in the sky, then
  blinking out momentarily and reappearing again. This would be
  like morphing, but involving the whole object instantaneously.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m11-006.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:42]
Butterfly Wings And UFOs

Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past




                                                          UFO Updates
                                      A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 11

                                                Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  From: Paul Scott Anderson <paulscottanderson.nul>
  Date: Fri, 11 Jun 2010 00:11:14 -0700
  Archived: Fri, 11 Jun 2010 06:15:35 -0400
  Subject: Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  >From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>
  >Date: Wed, 9 Jun 2010 11:49:25 -0300
  >Subject: Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  >I've paid attention. Unlike yourself, I'm not new at this.
  >Nothing that has been put forward as evidence has convinced me
  >of anything.
  >As you learn, William, you will find that the marvels are in the
  >small but inexplicable details, not in the flambouyant such as
  >crop circles and explainable spirals in a Norwegian sky.
  >Some CCs are good artwork in the style of graffitti but they
  >aren't proved as being associatied with the phenomenon that
  >interests me and which is the focus of this list.
  >I'm sure there are Lists out that that do waste their time on
  >crop circles. Show me some good trace cases, not pretty
  >graffitti.

  Don, Kathy et al,
  I'm not new at this either. I've been researching the phenomenon
  since the early 90s, and starting CCCRN here in Canada in 1995.
  I've been to quite a few Canadian formations and William makes
  very valid points. I've tried explaining that to this list
  myself before...
  It is the "small details" that often suggest whether a formation
  is an obviously man-made or not. Have you read _all_ of the
  available reports from BLT, etc.? Some of the most interesting
  formations have been simpler ones, including in Canada. And
  again, for reference, there have been several qualified
  scientists in different fields working with BLT since the late
  90s, _not_ just Levengood (who is now no longer associated with
  BLT, for some time now).
  Regarding stalk nodes, the "exploded" ones have sometimes been
  found in _still-standing_ plants around the perimeter of a
  circle and inside it, but not further out in the field. How does
  "just flattened plants laying out in the sun" explain that?
  We've done some simple tests of mechanically flattening cattle
  corn stalks near some existing formations and checking them
  periodically after. At least in these cases, being exposed to
  the sun (and it was usually hot weather) had no effect on them.
  There is also the historical evidence. There _are_ reports of
  formations going back decades and even centuries before Doug &
  Dave. Mostly simpler circles and rings, but also some geometric
  ones. Often with similar characteristics to circles we see now,
  such as standing centres of stalks, _not_ just crushed or burned
  landing trace-type cases. You had said before that all earlier
  "crop circles" were actually classic landing trace cases. But
  there is evidence otherwise. The circular and geometric
  Staffordshire ones below from 1686 were reported as being up to
  fifty yards in diameter - landing traces? I doubt it. Reported
  by a chemistry professor at Oxford, btw. Look at his diagrams.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m11-008.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:43]
Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  Ditto for the ones in Nature magazine in 1880.
  A Natural History of Staffordshire, 1686 (circles, rings,
  spirals and squares):
  http://www.bltresearch.com/otherfacts.php
  Nature magazine, 1880 (swirled circles with standing centres):
  http://www.cccrn.ca/cccrn/cropcircles101.html
  The Secret History of Crop Circles (excellent book and yes, I've
  read it):
  http://www.cropcircleconnector.com/anasazi/secretbook98.html
  Farmers have also first-handedly recounted circles, etc. going
  back several decades, well before the start of the "modern"
  phenomenon in the late 1970s/early 1980s, in a number of
  countries including Canada, USA, Germany, England, etc.
  I agree that many, maybe even most, of the current "fancy"
  formations are being made by "artists" as they call themselves,
  based on the physical examination, but they are just copying an
  already-existing phenomenon and building on it.

                                                         Secrecy News -- 06/10/10
  From: Steven Aftergood <saftergood.nul>

  Subject: Secrecy News -- 06/10/10

  SECRECY NEWS
  from the FAS Project on Government Secrecy
  Volume 2010, Issue No. 49
  June 10, 2010
  Secrecy News Blog:
  http://www.fas.org/blog/secrecy/

  **    DECLASSIFICATION AND THE "CRISIS" IN INTEL HISTORY
  **    GRID PROTECTION AND CYBERSECURITY
  **    TIME OUT

  DECLASSIFICATION AND THE "CRISIS" IN INTEL HISTORY
  The ongoing failure to establish a robust, reliable and
  productive declassification program is steadily eroding the
  study of intelligence history and may lead to the collapse of
  the entire field, one intelligence historian told the National
  Security Agency last month.
  "I don't think it's an exaggeration to say that we're at a
  crisis point in the study of intelligence history in general,
  and signals intelligence history in particular; because there
  is a very real question of whether any serious historians
  outside of the intelligence community are going to continue
  trying to research and understand and write about this subject
  at all," said author Stephen Budiansky in an invited lecture at
  the National Cryptologic Museum at Fort Meade on May 24.
  "The critical mass of scholars willing to invest the
  considerable energy required to master the technicalities of a
  complex and often difficult-to-understand subject is dwindling
  in the face of the impossibility of making a career in a field
  where the primary sources -- notably nearly all documents
  relating to the post-World War II period -- are locked away and
  no longer forthcoming."
  "As my fellow intelligence historian David Alvarez recently
  remarked to me, Dave Kahn [author of the pioneering book 'The
  Codebreakers'] may have the unique distinction of having created
  an entire new field of study, watched it blossom, and lived to
  see its demise," Mr. Budiansky said.
  "Alvarez said with only slight exaggeration that almost no one
  is working in the field of intelligence history any more. 'Even
  the crazies seem to have lost energy,' he said. He was recently
  on a panel to award a prominent prize for the best paper in any
  aspect of cryptologic history. Well past the deadline, they had
  received no entries at all."
  The main thrust of Mr. Budiansky's lecture, entitled "What's the
  Use of Cryptologic History?" (and not yet published), was not a
  plea for favoritism toward intelligence historians, but rather
  an argument for the importance of intelligence history -- to the
  general understanding of history, and to the practice of
  intelligence itself.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m11-010.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:43]
Secrecy News -- 06/10/10


  As it happens, a new effort to expedite the declassification of
  historical records is now underway at the new National
  Declassification Center. The Center has been tasked by
  President Obama with eliminating the backlog of more than 400
  million pages of classified records that are more than 25 years
  old by the end of 2013.
  Millions of newly declassified pages should be publicly
  available by the end of this month and each month thereafter,
  said Assistant Archivist Michael Kurtz on a conference call on
  June 4.
  This is a well-intentioned effort that will almost certainly
  yield a significant increase in public access to declassified
  records. But it also seems biased towards secrecy in two
  unfortunate ways.
  First, the review of the backlog will be conducted on a
  Pass/Fail basis, Mr. Kurtz said. That means that if a document
  contains any classified information at all, even a single word
  or number, the entire document will be withheld from release.
  This approach may be necessary in order to gain some traction on
  the enormous backlog and to avoid getting bogged down in
  details. But the regrettable consequence is that none of the
  unclassified contents of many partly classified documents will
  be disclosed through this process. (The documents may be
  redacted for release at a later time through a Freedom of
  Information Act request or through a subsequent declassification
  review.)
  Second, the documents that do pass the review and are
  declassified will be subjected to two quality control audits to
  ensure that no classified information has inadvertently passed
  through. One audit will be performed by the Archives and a
  second audit will be done by the Department of Energy. On the
  other hand, however, there will be no audit of withheld records
  to ensure that no unclassified record has been unnecessarily
  kept secret. In effect, the process is tilted towards
  minimizing disclosures of classified information rather than
  maximizing disclosures of unclassified information.
  The National Archives has prepared a draft prioritization plan
  to guide its declassification activities, and has invited public
  input on the plan. A public forum on the subject will be held on
  June 23.
  http://www.archives.gov/declassification/prioritization-plan.html

  GRID PROTECTION AND CYBERSECURITY
  The House of Representatives yesterday passed the "Grid
  Reliability and Infrastructure Defense Act" which is intended to
  bolster that national electric grid against terrorist attacks,
  cyber threats, electromagnetic pulse weapons and solar storms.
  The Act authorizes the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission to
  issue emergency orders to protect critical electric
  infrastructure, and to take other measures to address current
  and potential vulnerabilities.
  "The electric grid's vulnerability to cyber and to other attacks
  is one of the single greatest threats to our national security,"
  said Rep. Ed Markey (D-MA), who introduced the bill.
  The floor debate on the bill was a somewhat jarring mix of
  prudent anticipation and extravagant doomsday warnings.
  "Some of us read the book 'The Road' [a post-apocalyptic tale by
  Cormac McCarthy]," said Rep. Fred Upton (R-MI). "Lots of
  different scenarios are out there. We need to be prepared.
  This bill moves us down that road."
  "Scientists tell us that the likelihood of a severe naturally
  occurring geomagnetic event capable of crippling our electric
  grid is 100 percent," said Rep. Bennie Thompson (D-Miss.). "It
  will happen; it is just a question of when."
  "If you believe intelligence sources, our grid is already
  compromised," advised Rep. Yvette Clarke (D-NY). See the June 9
  floor debate here:


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m11-010.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:43]
Secrecy News -- 06/10/10


  http://www.fas.org/irp/congress/2010_cr/grid.html
  The Journal of National Security Law & Policy has just published
  a special issue dedicated to cybersecurity, with fifteen papers
  on various aspects of the issue. From various perspectives,
  they address what is known about the nature of the threat,
  current vulnerabilities, the role of the federal government, and
  policy options that are under consideration.
  http://jnslp.com/read/vol4no1.asp

  Once again, the fears that someone has leaked major secrets to
  the public via the wikileaks.com website, the Pentagon is on the
  hunt for the website's founder, Mr. Julian Assange.
  http://tinyurl.com/2vtls3z
  Wikileaks has been the bane of the existence of global crooks
  for quite a while now.
  It's very profitable for whistleblowers nowadays because the web
  can reach tens of millions of people who should be warned. No
  more cases of vital data being blocked by news services or
  government spooks in bed with big business or covering the hides
  of sleazy politicians. Nowadays if you mess up on a grand scale
  it'll end up broadcast all over the known universe before your
  next credit card statement arrives in the mail.
  So where are the UFO whistleblowers? I've mentioned this in an
  earlier post of a few days ago. Why has not one major UFO secret
  been revealed via Wikileaks? Surely the truth regarding UFOs and
  related phenom would so shake the foundations of global criminal
  evil that just one brave soul would come forward and forever
  change the history of humanity.
  So why am I hearing crickets chirping as I wait?

  Best,
  Greg

  this I mean the exploded nodules etc, are they found in plain
  circles that could be a depression made by some landed object or
  are they found in the more artistictic formations? To what
  degree?

                                                Weird 10 UFO Conference Speakers
  From: Philip Mantle <philip.nul>
  Date: Fri, 11 Jun 2010 17:16:25 +0100
  Archived: Fri, 11 Jun 2010 16:33:47 -0400
  Subject: Weird 10 UFO Conference Speakers

  Paranormal & UFO Conference 2010: Weird 10 Wiltshire s Premier
  Conference Event
  Keeping Wiltshire Weird!
  Weird 10 is proud to present twelve well known speakers,
  researchers, authors and TV celebrities in the UFO and
  Paranormal subject this August. The event will be held over two
  days at the atmospheric Athenaeum Victorian theatre in
  Warminster, Wiltshire. Nestling in the weirdest county in the
  UK, the town was once crowned the UFO capitol of the World
  You can expect a fantastic weekend of talks and presentations
  all hosted by the well liked and respected Malcolm Robinson.
  Malcolm will introduce household names like Nick Pope and Dr.
  Ciaran O Keeffe, as well as popular researchers such as Andy
  Russell, Dr. David Clarke, Andy Roberts and Wal Thornhill.
  With such a diverse guest list many different subjects and
  theories will be covered from the Supernatural in Warfare,
  plasma UFOs and Paranormal investigation to alternative theories
  of the cosmic standard model and the Rendlesham Forest Incident.
  There is something for everyone at the event regardless of your
  belief!
  We also offer a daily coach service from Swindon which is
  ideally located with major rail and road links. With hotels at a
  premium in Warminster why not stay in Swindon and let us take
  you to and from the event?
  Free copies of Paranormal Magazine will be on offer and large
  merchant stall areas will be stocked with all kinds of
  paranormal related items. The event will also launch two books
  by authors Andy Roberts and Brian Allan.
  An open question and answer session on Saturday is followed by
  Free live music in the evening by 'The Programme Initiative,' an
  innovated band that mixes stunning visuals with their music and
  celebrates a post-disclosure world. The evening ends with a
  chance to join the legendary UFO Warminster sky watch held at
  Cradle Hill on the outskirts of the town.
  Speakers: Kevin Goodman, Wal Thornhill, Nick Pope, David Clarke,
  Andy Roberts, Ciaran O Keeffe, Andrew Collins, Brian Allan,
  ASSAP, Ross Hemsworth.
  Special Guest: Philip Mantle, Editor of UFO Matrix magazine,
  sponsors of Weird 10.
  You can also save over 10% on the price of a weekend pass by
  purchasing your tickets now.


  Date 21st and 22nd August. 9:15am 5pm both days.
  Ticket Prices: =A335 for a weekend pass if bought in advance (over
  10% discount), =A320 per day at the door.

                                               What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Sat, 12 Jun 2010 05:09:38 EDT
  Archived: Sat, 12 Jun 2010 19:56:00 -0400
  Subject: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  I'm sitting here using a computer and communications system
  rumored to have been created in part from back engineered ET
  technology.
  We're all aware of the legends and statements by those who
  disclose UFOs, that many of the devices and technologies we use
  on a day to day basis are the result of such back engineering.
  However, with the world once again in the crapper economically,
  our being on the verge of another World War, massive oil spills
  that further endanger life and property, I ask the question what
  good was this back engineering?
  It's like you know if you cage monkeys all they'll do is throw
  their crap at each other and us visitors to the zoo and if you
  up the ante and give them technology like a shovel or a broom
  they'll just use that shovel or broom to better throw their crap
  at each other and we visitors to the zoo.
  I guess since as a species we're already our worst enemy and
  anything you give us we'll use to ruin whatever we have so maybe
  it's best we not have official disclosure. Sure it couldn't
  hurt but it sure couldn't help.

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com

  >development. It was all straight-forward engineering. No

  >technology that was 9 microns in width and worked on it until it
  >became sub-micron in size.

  Robert,
  Thanks for the reply.
  If you've been in on the development of the PC then you should
  be familiar with IBM's main manufacturing plant in my old
  hometown of Poughkeepsie, NY. My kin have been working at IBM
  since it was started. Thousands and thousands of family and
  friends at that company over the decades.
  In addition to their main plant being in Poughkeepsie, the
  center of business in the Hudson Valley, there has also been a
  decades long UFO problem. I'm not talking a few instances but
  events so long going and public it's become part of the
  landscape.
  I've had close friends and family just up and tell me about UFO
  related associations with IBM and the dozens of research and
  development labs in the region. So when researchers, witnesses
  and such allude to us having back engineered such things or
  having been influenced by such it weighs heavily as true.
  It's the application of these technologies I'm giggling about
  here.

  Hi Eleanor,
  The American Computer Company's President was using the idea of
  leaked Alien technology to draw attention to his web site many
  years ago, alleging that he had seen proof that the transistor
  was based on technology that had been presented to the
  scientists involved in the development, but this had no proof
  nor provenance to support it. Indeed, it smelled of a raw
  attempt to attract customers and the President was very abrasive
  when interviewed. That story begins here:
  http://www.acsa.net/alien/acc-historical/.
  What was the impetus in 1948 to try using silicon, rather than
  germanium, in the transistor experiment? Prior to that, I
  believe there was a lot of effort to create a device using
  germanium. But it was unstable and difficult to manufacture, so
  it didn't get far. It's also interesting that the patent for the
  Transistor was initially rejected because it didn't contain
  enough detail regarding its development. In other words, the
  Patent Office was concerned that this patent have a clear line
  of development, and the initial application failed to explain
  it. Was the Patent application back engineered from the
  transistor they'd developed, or the other way around?
  Then, of course, there are the Corso allegations that he helped
  to promote the development of integrated circuits using Roswell
  crash material that he had access to at the Pentagon. But this
  is a tale without provenance nor evidence to support it, and it
  rests on the word of the now deceased Col. Corso. FYI, I worked
  with some of the very early Classified integrated circuits (ICs)
  that were used in a military device used during the Viet Nam
  war. This was prior to 1972 and a number of years before the
  first Apple was create in a California garage. These ICs were
  very new technology at the time and didn't look like the
  traditional IC that was sold a few years later by Allied Radio


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m14-005.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:51]
Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  and Radio Shack.
  Greg asks a rhetorical question that is not really ufological in
  nature, but raises the irony of the situation if we indeed have
  been aided in our technological development by Aliens. None of
  our technology will help us, regardless of the source, if we
  lose the power grid, or the global economy collapses. There are
  many reasons to be concerned, as the Gulf is poisoned by leaking
  oil, and the climate appears to be in a state of change, and
  resources are becoming more scarce, which leaves a narrow path
  to follow to avoid disaster. Unfortunately, I'm not sure anyone
  knows, or can agree on, what that path is.
  The Chinese curse, "May you live in interesting times", may well
  be the case.

  Steve

  Thanks Steven, you've made my points again.
  I disagree that resources are scarce. The only scarcity is our
  inability to take responsibility and forge ahead. The Universe
  offers no scarcities. There's an abundance of everything but it
  challenges us to go get it.
  At this point in human history we have the collective knowledge,
  ideas, evaluations and practicals of almost every civilization
  and culture encountered.
  Millions of years of data regarding our own planet and fast
  gathering of data of worlds within our own solar system. Yet,
  history has shown no matter what we humans discover or engage
  in, it is inevitable that it will result in a degradation to a
  level of simple primate behavior.
  I've joked with friends and during my stand-up comedy routine
  that animals only hang around us humans because they think we're
  disadvantaged/special needs cases because we can't walk on all
  fours. They feel they need to help us out. I've joked how horses
  are really comedians at heart as they need to get us to ride
  them in order to get around and it makes all the other animals
  laugh. Look at the poor disadvantaged human on my back! Isn't it
  funny? He's got four legs but doesn't know it! C'mon dog, cat,
  eagle, isn't this a riot?
  As an example of our foibles, I was up late writing tonight and
  watched the gangster classic from 1932 Scarface. The movie
  starts out berating the government to do something about
  organized crime. There's a brilliant speech given during the
  movie about the fate of America should such goings on continue
  without the pubic rising up and taking responsibility. That
  speech is as timely today as it was 80 years ago and we still
  have one massive criminal act after another but on a global
  scale.
  I don't think we have or will make it. Whatever we were supposed
  to be or dreamed of being won't happen unless an act of God or
  breakthrough in some technology comes forth to orient the core
  beingness of mankind so we don't end up killing everything on
  the planet.
  Religious mythos says we were originally created as the
  caretakers of life on this world. We went astray. Whose to say
  another group won't or hasn't already been designed to take our
  place? It' apparent to me that life force manipulates matter not
  the other way around. As we speak and battle and quarrel and
  follow our hormones, the force of life is probably working out
  another solution or has already and we're just too low on the
  scale to perceive and acknowledge it.
  So like I asked, back engineering is no good as all it does is
  provides technologies that we'll use to satiate our own monsters
  from the ID.
  Don't be surprised if Caretaker 2.0 shows up sometime soon and
  gives us the boot.

  From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
  Date: Mon, 14 Jun 2010 15:04:31 -0300
  Archived: Tue, 15 Jun 2010 08:54:48 -0400
  Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  >From: John Rimmer <johnrimmer.nul>

  >Date: Mon, 14 Jun 2010 08:52:16 -0700 (PDT)

  >So the aliens had to show us how to build computers!
  >Yet another thread on UpDates based on the premise that humans
  >are a stupid, unenlightened race, incapable of tying our own
  >shoelaces without help from superior aliens.
  >Why are some people here so frightened of the idea that we are
  >perfectly capable of doing things for ourselves and are actually
  >a very ingenious people? Is it because they're so worried about
  >having to take responsibilty for their own lives?
  >
  >How sad.
  Gotta agree. I've never been much for the back engineering
  theory. Shockley, et al, was driven by Bell Lab's need for
  something that switched 0/1 faster than 400 times a second-
  pretty much the limit of the vacumn tube. This was driven by the
  telephone computer systems of the late 40s and early 50s in
  areas like New York City where millions of telephones could be
  in use at a given moment. The human operators just could not
  keep up with that.
  They came up with the transistor after experimenting with
  various materials and ways of using very low voltages to control
  higher (but still low) voltages but at speeds of 10,000 times a
  second. They came up with the transistor. Simple trial and error
  the same as Edison's techs did. You didn't really think Edison
  did all of that himself did you? Mythology for the movies.
  I think John is right here. We are pretty bright-at least the
  parts of the world that doesn't let religious horse manure to
  get in the way of building an intelligent and educated society.
  I also don't subscribe to the notion that we are a violent
  species. Any species capable of evolving did so at the expense
  of other species, either by eating them or dominating them.
  That's the nature of the beast. Eventually we learn to control
  that and are still evolving or at least few couple of billion of
  us are.

  Don Ledger

  Hi John,
  I think it's somewhat sad that you've interpreted this
  theorizing as "fear", but I suppose you have your
  interpretations to support it.
  I've yet to see any real evidence that any technology was based
  on retro-engineering, but that doesn't prevent one from
  speculating, when the actual development process isn't clear and
  leaves it open to question.
  The issue of the transistor's patent application being rejected
  when it was first submitted is a subject discussed among
  employees, but I should give the impression that this was a
  single unusual case.
  On the other hand, it's probably the most famous among those who
  tell tales of exciting Patent Office news... <grin>. But I
  mention it because this isn't a story they tell in conjunction
  with alien technology or reverse engineering.
  I guess the poor un-enlightened ufologists will have to throw
  that connection up against the wall and see if it sticks.

                                           Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Mon, 14 Jun 2010 22:45:09 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 15 Jun 2010 09:08:48 -0400
  Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  >From: John Rimmer <johnrimmer.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Mon, 14 Jun 2010 08:52:16 -0700 (PDT)
  >Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  >So the aliens had to show us how to build computers!
  >Yet another thread on UpDates based on the premise that humans
  >are a stupid, unenlightened race, incapable of tying our own
  >shoelaces without help from superior aliens.
  >Why are some people here so frightened of the idea that we are
  >perfectly capable of doing things for ourselves and are actually
  >a very ingenious people? Is it because they're so worried about
  >having to take responsibilty for their own lives?
  >How sad.
  So you would say we are an enlightened species. What evidence do
  you have for that?
  With relatively little effort today, we can discover that all is
  not as we are led to believe. For example, authorities like
  mainstream academia don't acknowledge, or are discouraged from
  acknowledging, the evidence for a much expanded history of the
  human race. There appears to be plenty of evidence to suggest
  that relatively high-level civilizations existed on earth more
  than 12000 years ago.
  If we are so smart, why isn't research funding to investigate
  this encouraged rather than discouraged. For example, the right
  to video evidence about the underwater structures near Bimini
  was signed over to National Geographic who then suppressed it.
  Graham Hancock has dived locations the world over to investigate
  similar underwater phenomena, and finds little mainstream
  interest in studying the evidence he's collected.
  We know about these things because we can see images and hear
  and read stories on-line. But official recognition that there is
  something worth investigating is not forthcoming, even though
  the implications are enormous. How enlightened is that? Why do
  we as a species allow that?
  Your contention that humanity doesn't need any help implies that
  we already recognize the full scope of human history. Since this
  is demonstrably not true, it follows that we must be in some
  kind of intellectual straight jacket, discouraged from thinking
  outside the box we are in. The flip side is that we are being
  led intellectually in an approved direction. This suggests that
  paradigm shifting technological advances like the invention of
  the transistor could well have been given by our keepers, or at
  least, allowed to occur unimpeded. If it had not been approved,
  it would have been suppressed like the evidence of civilizations
  existing 12000 years ago.
  I agree there are ingenious people among us. But often in the
  sciences, authority figures decide what is true, and all genius
  is not recognized. Funding pressure is even applied to not do
  research of certain kinds. For example, the aether was declared


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m15-010.shtml[10/12/2011 23:15:58]
Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  to not exist even though people are now realizing that the
  analysis of experimental evidence used to make that decision was
  flawed. Recently, certain stubborn scientists are resisting that
  declaration, and are thus able to develop theory and evidence
  for novel forces in nature. But there is still very little
  official recognition of this evidence.
  Back in the mid 20th century, physicists were actually doing
  empirical research on anti-gravity and other novel forms of
  propulsion. Following the publication of the Biefield-Brown
  effect and its possible role in new propulsion systems, further
  research of this nature went underground probably into research
  programs of the military/industrial complex. Again, it's
  knowledge that does not fit into the box designed for the rest
  of us.
  The rest of us have to tolerate the limitations of the box in
  which we live. We continue using oil in spite of the extremely
  negative environmental effects, when there is reason to think
  that viable alternative energy sources exist outside the box. We
  are led to believe that greedy capitalism is good for us because
  it promotes competition. But the same cheap energy source could
  release us to develop distributed systems of production where we
  each produce some of our basic needs, and perhaps return to a
  barter system where we take care of each other. But that would
  reduce our dependence on the keepers of the box. Can't let that
  happen, so the oil-based economy persists.
  So I find your implied assertion that humanity is enlightened a
  little hard to accept. Most of us really have little idea of
  what appears to be going on in the greater scheme of things. If
  we did, we would be in a much better position to, as you say,
  take responsibility for our own lives.

  William

In The Hunt For Planets Who Owns The Data




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 16

                                        In The Hunt For Planets Who Owns The Data
  From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
  Date: Tue, 15 Jun 2010 14:28:32 -0300
  Archived: Wed, 16 Jun 2010 06:36:48 -0400
  Subject: In The Hunt For Planets Who Owns The Data

  The New York Times
  June 14, 2010
  In The Hunt For Planets, Who Owns The Data?
  By Dennis Overbye
  We are about to find out just how generous nature really is.
  On Tuesday, astronomers operating NASA’s Kepler spacecraft will
  release a list of about 350 stars newly suspected of harboring
  planets, including five systems with multiple candidate planets.
  That data could dramatically swell the inventory of alien
  worlds, which now stands at 461, none of them habitable by the
  likes of us.
  Astronomers everywhere, who have been waiting since Kepler’s
  launch in March 2009 to get their hands on this data, will be
  rushing to telescopes to examine these stars in the hopes of
  advancing the grand quest of finding Earthlike planets capable
  of harboring life out there.
  But a lot of attention has been paid in astronomical circles
  over the past few months to what the Kepler team will not be
  saying. By agreement with NASA, the team is holding back data on
  its 400 brightest and best planet candidates, which the
  astronomers intend to observe themselves over a busy summer.
  [More...]
  www.nytimes.com/2010/06/15/science/space/15kepler.html?th&emc=th

  >I think quite a lot of people are actually frightened of the
  >idea that there is no friendly guiding figure 'out there',
  >whether its God, gods or some nice guys who tell us how to make
  >transistors.
  >As people here will know, I disgree with the ETH as an
  >explantaion for UFOs, but actually I have no strong feelings one
  >way or the other as to whether there are other inhabited
  >planets. But when, playing Devil's Advocate, I argue that we are
  >alone in the universe, it's remarkable just how upset people
  >get!
  >So, yes, I think people are frightened of the idea that we're on
  >our own. Just imagine, that big old universe, and we're the only
  >people in it!

  John,
  You had my sympathy in criticizing the 'humans got it all from
  aliens' nonsense, but maybe you should have cashed in at that
  point.
  "Just imagine", indeed. A limited imagination is indicated if
  you find it remarkable that people might be disturbed to learn
  that intelligent life only occurs on this infinitesimal
  flyspeck, notwithstanding the fact that, by every indication of
  physical law, and more recently by direct observation, similar
  planets and conditions should be widespread in the universe.
  Sounds like a Twilight Zone episode to me. Frankly, I'd like to
  hear a coherent explanation of such a circumstance... hopefully,
  a non-frightening one, i.e., one that doesn't involve "God,
  gods, or some nice guys".

  Regards
  Mike

Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

                                           Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  From: Cathy Reason <CathyM.nul>
  Date: Wed, 16 Jun 2010 12:54:10 +0100
  Archived: Wed, 16 Jun 2010 08:58:07 -0400
  Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  >From: John Rimmer <johnrimmer.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Tue, 15 Jun 2010 15:27:55 +0000 (GMT)
  >Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  >I think quite a lot of people are actually frightened of the
  >idea that there is no friendly guiding figure 'out there',
  >whether its God, gods or some nice guys who tell us how to make
  >transistors.
  >As people here will know, I disgree with the ETH as an
  >explantaion for UFOs, but actually I have no strong feelings one
  >way or the other as to whether there are other inhabited
  >planets. But when, playing Devil's Advocate, I argue that we are
  >alone in the universe, it's remarkable just how upset people
  >get!
  >So, yes, I think people are frightened of the idea that we're on
  >our own. Just imagine, that big old universe, and we're the only
  >people in it!
  >Scaaaaary!!!
  This seems to me to take no account of the way cultural
  representations of ETI have changed over the years. The picture
  of a benevolent, technologically sophisticated, morally evolved
  ETI hasn't had much currency since the 1970s - and if it hadn't
  been for Carl Sagan, it might never have gained much currency at
  all.
  The contemporary picture seems to be some sort of partly
  insectile, partly mechanical intelligence with a hive-mind
  structure, a rapacious attitude to its environment and no
  discernible moral values at all - the writing of Alastair
  Reynolds would be a case in point. Whether this picture is
  really a projection of some characteristics of our own species
  we don't much like to admit to, is of course another interesting
  question.
  In fact with the intellectual dominance of rationalist humanism
  and its increasingly nationalistic attitude toward the human
  collective, the notion of an extraterrestrial intelligence which
  is both technologically and morally superior to the human race
  could be seen as an extremely unpalatable threat to racial pride
  and status.

  Cathy

  >If "we" are actually a very ingenious people, then "we" are also
  >a moronically stupid people, just as "we" are a generous,
  >altruistic people, as well as a selfish, venal and small-minded
  >people.
  >These sorts of epithets are properly the qualities of
  >individuals and are completely meaningless when applied to
  >abstract collectives. Unless of course you want to argue that
  >humanity is actually some sort of hive organism - which would be
  >an interesting discussion in itself.

  Hi,
  You might consider looking at a subject called sociology, it
  deals with human collective behaviour.
  As for back-engineering, I think Stan is grasping at straws. At
  best he can say is that observations 'may' have helped in the
  design of the Apollo Command Module. Where is the evidence? He's
  just speculating.
  UFO sightings have certainly inspired people to build disc-
  shaped craft, and come up with all sorts of exotic designs, but
  that is not the same as back-engineering from an actual crashed
  or recovered flying saucer.
  Why haven't boffins back-engineered teleportation devices, star
  ship propulsion systems, invisibility cloaks and the more exotic
  technological claims of the contactees and abductees? If they
  were any good we'd be abducting aliens in some far flung galaxy
  by now.

  Nigel

                                                                  Another View
  From: Ed Gehrman <egehrman.nul>
  Date: Wed, 16 Jun 2010 08:03:34 -0700

  Subject: Another View

  From The Day After Roswell by Philip Corso - Page 172:
  "Maybe the Roswell crash was also the mechanism for
  successfully implanting a completely alien non-human life
  form that survives from host to host like a virus, a digital
  Ebola that we humans will carry to another planet, someday.
  Or what if an enemy wanted to implant the perfect spying or
  sabotage mechanism into a culture? The implantation of the
  micro-chip-based circuit into our technology would be the
  perfect method. Was it (the debris) implanted as sabotage or as
  something akin to the gift of fire? Or like poisoned fruit
  dropping from the trees into the playground."

  Ed

  as we as a species are still as violent and accident prone as

  but far too many of us are still to delusional to come in from
  out of the hurricane.

  disaster manmade or natural and the first thing we have to do is
  protect that system from virus and malware producing hooligans.
  We're faulty at the core either by our software/beliefs or our
  hardware/genetics. If you introduced flying cars we'd have
  drunks in flying cars and flying carjacking.

  From: Steven Kaeser <steve.nul>
  Date: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 08:15:14 -0400
  Archived: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 08:36:02 -0400
  Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  >From: Nigel Watson <nigelwatson1.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Wed, 16 Jun 2010 13:51:37 +0100
  >Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  <snip>
  >Why haven't boffins back-engineered teleportation devices, star ship
  propulsion systems, invisibility cloaks and the more exotic technological
  claims of the contactees and abductees? If they were >any good we'd be
  abducting aliens in some far flung galaxy by now.

  Nigel,
  Now you're adding human speculation to the mix...
  You assume that we'd recognize the technology involved in any
  kind of alien craft we might stumble across? Without an
  understanding of the science behind the devices you've assumed
  would be part of the mix, I'm not sure that we could identify
  components or their purpose, or even stand of ghost's chance in
  Hell of figuring it out. I tend to agree with Kaku that it's
  unlikely that an Alien race that was travelling across the
  vastness of space would have much in common or be that
  interested in civilizations that hadn't reached that level, so
  this humanization occurs on both sides of this genre.
  But not everyone believes that the provenance of some
  technologies and how they were developed is fully disclosed;
  and, in at least one case questions were raised causing a
  backfill of information generated after the fact. So this is a
  discussion that will continue, while most of us wait for some
  sort of documented proof.

  Steve

                                            Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 10:13:14 -0500
  Archived: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 11:57:06 -0400
  Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  >From: Nigel Watson <nigelwatson1.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 13:57:36 +0100
  >Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  >>From: Steven Kaeser <steve.nul>
  >>To: <post.nul>
  >>Date: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 08:15:14 -0400
  >>Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  >The problem with this line of thinking is that the aliens can do
  >anything we can imagine and beyond...
  >As a consequence their activities are in the realm of god-like
  >magic, which means that human science or study will never
  >discover if they exist or not.
  This is considerably overstating the case, or at least
  needlessly simplifying a more complicated matter. A number of
  variables need to be considered - to start with attention to how
  far ahead of us a civilization would have to be in order to
  develop technology which makes travel between stars doable. It's
  conceivable that such a breakthrough doesn't require (by earthly
  counting) thousands of years, or even hundreds, but perhaps
  decades. A technology a few decades ahead of us, or even a few
  hundred, isn't going to look magical, just advanced and not
  immediately understandable. That's pretty much how the hard-core
  UFO cases look.
  >We could either be glimpsing alien activities or we might just
  >be chasing 'alien fog' generated by our own vivid imaginations
  >and a strong belief that we are not alone in the universe.
  The "alien fog" is the one that confuses astronomers who
  strongly believe, without evidence, that we are not alone in the
  universe but who reject, without knowing anything about the
  relevant evidence (try to get a dismissive astronomer to discuss
  the RB-47 case) for possible ET visitation. A question for the
  psychosociologists, I should think.

  Jerry Clark

  system. Bits and pieces can be helpful.
  There were wind tunnel tests run on disc-shapes in late 1947.
  One reason I spoke about large programs was that they can cost
  huge amounts of money... not basement inventor kind of stuff.

  Stan Friedman

  was available to society in general, not cloistered scientific
  thinkers and entrenched religious dogma. What John Harney wrote
  about is pretty much what I've read over the years about the
  thinkers from Aristotle to Wells.
  I will take John to task over one mention of his and that is his
  claiming that the popular view of the "space brothers" by
  abductees is that they are benevolent. From my reading and my
  own interaction with abductees -a nd I'm sure John Velez will
  agree with this - is that there is nothing at all benevelent
  about their abductors. This benevolency idea is promoted by the


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m17-011.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:07]
Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  fringe that are in it for whatever 'spirirual' value they can
  get from it rather than actually being abducted. This appliesto
  the UFO phenomenon. But as usual those who debunk will reference
  the woo-woo side every time in an attempt to make light of what
  is and has been a serious situation and still unexplained.
  The latest ploy is that the study of the phenomenon has been
  endless decades of speculation. John could not resist falling
  into the same old prose as his peer group and so his part three
  is titled Deserts Of Arid Speculation, where he takes those
  actually doing the grunge work to task for not supplying him
  with answers while he and others sit on the sidelines casting
  aspersions but doing nothing themselves. This is a normal but
  selfish trait of society. They react but do not act.
  John follows with - again with fringe references - that any
  speculation that there might be some life beyond that on Earth
  displays similar thinking to that of religion which of course is
  not so. Religion speculates on the premise of an all powerful
  entity that holds sway over your afterlife with contractual
  obligations built into the bargin. Keep paying your dues believe
  and support our ideals and we'll see that you get to the happy
  hunting grounds, heaven or that great horde of virgins in the
  sky. Disbelieve and you go to the bad place - which one comedian
  questioned wondering why the bad place's superintendent would go
  along with the deity's wishes and make death miserable for those
  who went against the deity when that superintendent did the same
  thing as well.
  Anyway, it certainly isn't my take on life eleswhere. The odds
  are in favor of same; the argument arises over distance of other
  life forms from Earth not its possibility of just existing.
  Harney stated," This is even more true of ufology, with the
  added complication that we can not always be certain of what the
  author is trying to tell us, because so many UFO authors find
  that they have to have their original works heavily larded with
  catch-penny sensationalism before their publishers will accept
  them." Speaking for Jenney Randles, David Clarke and Andy
  Roberts, this is a terrible accusation and in my own case
  absolutely untrue.

  Don Ledger

  Don't even get me started on the Space Brothers crowd.
  All in all what we've got here is the same thing we've started
  out with and that's lots of astounding anecdotal evidence, some
  physical evidence that says something interacted with our
  environment at the place of a sighting, lots of government
  documents that prove beyond any shadow of a doubt that there was
  far more interest in UFOs than the public was told, lots of
  highly credible whistleblowers and lots of photos, films, and
  videos of which none are rock solid conclusive.
  We're still in the foggy zone 60 plus years into this phenom.
  The question I'm posing is that if there has been any back
  engineering it's either not much or to no use. No one saw any
  cosmic or high tech intervention during the 911 attacks, nor any
  help with the massive oil spills of late, tsunamis, earthquakes
  etc.. Sure are lots of stories about cosmic intervention in the
  old texts from ancient civilizations but I'm betting that the
  proliferation of one invention put a stop to any more so called
  visits from the gods:
  The Gun.
  Seems as soon as shootin' irons became popular there has been a
  drastic drop in divine visitations even when there were some
  astounding opportunities to do so such as WWI and WWII. Why?
  Because there's one thing you might want to put down as an
  axiom:
  Bullets ain't superstitious.
  I sat here this week watching hours of documentaries about the
  space race during the '60s. We went from putting chimps in
  rockets to men on the moon in 9 years. I didn't see any alien
  beings showing up lending a hand although some allude to that
  there was but again no solid evidence or proof. Just anecdotes
  and stonewalling. I sit here marvelling at what the scientists,
  engineers, and especially the astronauts went through to achieve
  their goals. Fifty years of manned space flight and some amazing
  technologies and discoveries about ourselves and our environment
  have come to light. I remember as a kid knowing every astronaut
  from the Gemini to the Space Shuttle programs and missions.
  Those folks are like sports stars to me. Only less than a


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m18-001.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:08]
Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  handful discuss UFOs though and I've met several astronauts and
  know a mess of folks who worked on the space program but no
  signs of any back engineering or cosmic intervention.
  I'll be the first however to say I've been told by highly
  credible people that there has been but not publicly known.
  These folks however seem to always fall short of the goal line
  after adamantly stating their cases. I'm waiting on two out of
  four witnesses with solid data to come forward. We'll see.
  Bottom line for me is that the more technology we have the more
  our base behaviors have. Sort of like that classic sci-fi movie
  Forbidden Planet. Whatever you get hold of so does your ID.
  If there's any real answer to all this it will have to come from
  the collective data from hard scientific investigation and that
  just might turn out to find out that we can't solve this issue,
  that it's always going to be a carrot on a stick scenario, a
  Sisyphus Effect where the boulder will always roll down that
  hill no matter how many times we roll it back up.

  Best,
  Greg

  >>Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  <snip>
  >>You might consider looking at a subject called sociology, it
  >>deals with human collective behaviour.
  >>As for back-engineering, I think Stan is grasping at straws. At
  >>best he can say is that observations 'may' have helped in the
  >>design of the Apollo Command Module. Where is the evidence? He's
  >>just speculating.
  >>UFO sightings have certainly inspired people to build disc-
  >>shaped craft, and come up with all sorts of exotic designs, but
  >>that is not the same as back-engineering from an actual crashed
  >>or recovered flying saucer.
  >>Why haven't boffins back-engineered teleportation devices, star
  >>ship propulsion systems, invisibility cloaks and the more exotic
  >>technological claims of the contactees and abductees? If they
  >>were any good we'd be abducting aliens in some far flung galaxy
  >>by now.
  >Nigel,
  >Perhaps you could read what I said, "Might have been
  >Stimulated".
  >Clearly a speculation, not given as a fact. No straw grasping.
  >I would probably bet on the samarium cobalt permanent magnets.
  >Back engineering does not necessarily mean duplicating an entire
  >system. Bits and pieces can be helpful.
  >There were wind tunnel tests run on disc-shapes in late 1947.
  >One reason I spoke about large programs was that they can cost
  >huge amounts of money... not basement inventor kind of stuff.
  Back in the days when the AFs were concerned about the UFO
  enigma, specifically where these things were constantly out
  running conventional aircraft, there was speculation that these
  were Soviet aircraft. I have no doubt that the classic UFO shape
  was seriously considered as to its influence on the
  maneuverability and speed of these anomalies.Therefore there
  were attempts at duplicating the shape if not the performance.
  Wind tunnel tests did indicate that the discoid shape is
  disirable for maneuvering and is a great lifting surface. Any
  kid with a frisbee could tell you that.
  The Avro-car is one example of design and experimentation that


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m18-002.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:09]
Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  proved worthless in practice. Others might have followed but if
  they did, no evidence - except in the uninformed minds of the
  "Top Secret aircraft are the culprit crowd" - has shown up to
  support any advances in that arena.
  We are still playing around with thrust to weight ratios; the
  operative word there being thrust; meaning props, rotors, jet
  props, jets, rockets ( solid, liquid fueled and nuclear concept
  and experimentation) ram jets and scram jets. The latter would
  be the recently flight flown but short flight experiment, the
  Waverider.
  Experiments have been performed using microwaves to ionize the
  leading edges and the the first 25 percent of a conventional
  jets wing to defeat air piling up creating a shock wave in front
  of the leading edge then traversing rearward over the wing and
  creating drag, the enemy of thrust. But this also creates a lot
  of weight and defeats the purpose of thrust versus drag by
  intoducing the weight and defeating lift. To defeat this you
  need more thrust meaning bigger engines and more fuel and then
  you are back to the weight versus lift factor once again.
  Advancements have been made using lighter material such as
  carbon fibers, fly by wire, and drag reduction through design
  improvements but in the end the aircraft are still being pushed
  along via jet thrust.
  Being a keen observer of all aircraft I could learn about since
  I was a 4 year old, nothing has shown up that was a surprise.
  The Waverider still operates on the same principle as the none
  electric J-3 Piper Cub that was built in the 1930s (but is still
  flying today) e.g. thrust versus drag, lift versus
  weight/gravity. The difference is that the J-3 flies around for
  about 3.5 hours on 10-12 gallons of fuel (weight about 92
  pounds) while the Waverider flies for about two minutes on a
  couple of tons of fuel; and both cover about the same distance.

  Don Ledger

                                  Both Sides Of The Abduction Fence [was: What Good
  From: Steven Kaeser <steve.nul>
  Date: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 14:31:01 -0400
  Archived: Fri, 18 Jun 2010 07:15:32 -0400
  Subject: Both Sides Of The Abduction Fence [was: What Good

  >From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>
  >Date: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 13:12:57 -0300
  >Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  <snip>
  >I will take John to task over one mention of his and that is his
  >claiming that the popular view of the "space brothers" by
  >abductees is that they are benevolent. From my reading and my
  >own interaction with abductees - and I'm sure John Velez will
  >agree with this - is that there is nothing at all benevelent
  >about their abductors. This benevolency idea is promoted by the
  >fringe that are in it for whatever 'spirirual' value they can
  >get from it rather than actually being abducted. This appliesto
  >the UFO phenomenon. But as usual those who debunk will reference
  >the woo-woo side every time in an attempt to make light of what
  >is and has been a serious situation and still unexplained.

  Don,
  John may or may not respond, but from what I've seen there are
  those who stand on both sides of that fence, and perhaps a few
  people sitting on top of it. In the DC region an attempt to work
  with a large group of abductees in the Washington in the late
  80s and early 90s, and it quickly became apparent that there
  were two camps among those who'd been abducted and they bickered
  and debated the question of the abductors being benevolent to
  the point where the conversation became emotional and heated. In
  the end, they blamed the ufologists and psychologists that had
  been brought into the mix and threw them out of the meetings
  completely.
  I know two abductees and neither view the situation in a
  positive light, but both also are completely resigned to the
  reality of it. But when one of them attempted to join in an
  Abductee group he found that this group of individuals had
  evolved into thinking of the entire experience as a positive
  one. I suspect that over time, this group evolved into a mental
  "safe place" that they could accept with less fear. My friend
  was asked to not come back because of his negativity in
  discussing the subject, which told me quite a bit about many in
  the field and the "experts" they work with.
  I would suspect that those who believe alien abduction is a
  positive thing are trying to reconcile their involvement, rather
  than looking for some spiritual satisfaction.

  Steve

An Abduction Theory That Takes The Cake




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 18

                                          An Abduction Theory That Takes The Cake
  From: Gre Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Fri, 18 Jun 2010 03:04:00 EDT
  Archived: Fri, 18 Jun 2010 07:21:47 -0400
  Subject: An Abduction Theory That Takes The Cake

  I thought I'd heard it all up until now.
  That UFO abductees had all sorts of disorders and then that
  abductees were reliving past life stuff and that there was post
  prenatal configurations of the unconscious mind.
  Now here's a new one:
  Mosquitoes.
  Yes, you read that right, mosquitoes. That UFO abductees
  are actually experiencing latent memories of mosquito
  bites. That when mosquitoes bite they create a psychological
  cascade of unconscious fears that pile up and up and manifest
  as delusions so real that we can't tell the difference.
  The similarities of bug eyed aliens with long probes extracting
  fluids is so traumatizing that the mind fills in the blanks,
  exaggerates and since some mosquito bites can cause delirium
  like as in malaria, abductees are misinterpreting the mosquito
  intrusions.
  Now we know mosquitoes have been the number one cause of death
  of humans since who knows when as they carry any number of
  diseases and do transmit genetic material from other animals but
  this explanation just went so over the top as a grasping of
  straws that I had to post it.
  Has anyone ever heard of this before?

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com

The Prime Directive? Non-Interference My Foot




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                      The Prime Directive? Non-Interference My Foot
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Fri, 18 Jun 2010 15:58:52 EDT
  Archived: Sat, 19 Jun 2010 07:19:51 -0400
  Subject: The Prime Directive? Non-Interference My Foot

  Recently on this List the issue of a Prime Directive ala the
  science fiction franchise Star Trek wherein aliens decide not to
  interfere with another world's goings on.
  That makes no sense whatsoever.
  Why would someone travel trillions of miles_not_to_interfere?
  That's like one of us taking a thousand mile journey to Las
  Vegas and not taking in the sights.
  Sorry, but if the stories from ages past of beings from the
  skies who did everything_except_interfere can be transposed as
  aliens from wherever, the only conclusion is that they've always
  interfered but have lightened up greatly due to the
  proliferation of our weapons based societies.
  Maybe that old routine where some super smart guy shows up
  claiming to be a god kind of got stymied when such brilliant
  scientists such as Mr. Smith and Mr. Wesson developed their
  technologies.
  Believe you me, if I was to travel trillions of miles to
  anywhere I'm going to be on interfering fool.

  Best,
  Greg

                                               Aliens Built The Great Pyramids - Not!
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Fri, 18 Jun 2010 16:05:06 EDT
  Archived: Sat, 19 Jun 2010 07:21:23 -0400
  Subject: Aliens Built The Great Pyramids - Not!

  Again, I'm sorry but I'm not buying it. I've tried to look at it
  but the stories that aliens built the Great Pyramids of Giza
  just has nothing to it.
  I've said it before and will say it again, there are often
  amongst the masses of humanity some extremely smart people.
  Sure for the most part they're delusional full of self absorbed
  importance ala delusions of grandeur, but they're still very,
  very smart people.
  Maybe it's that one in a million that shines bright and doesn't
  get strung up, burned at the stake, or tossed into a mental
  institution that gets through and does something spectacular
  that others, who are too arrogant to grant others the gift of
  superior intellect, just can't fathom.
  The 20th Century had some awesome super geniuses and it could be
  due to genetics, resources, nutrition or any combination thereof
  but without any physical proof, stating aliens from the stars or
  whatever did this or that is still anecdotal.
  I'll be the first to say some witnesses I have that are super
  credible state the opposite regarding certain 20th Century
  technologies but without physical proof they're still just
  barking at the shadows.
  We'll see.
  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com

  >positive thing are trying to reconcile their involvement, rather
  >than looking for some spiritual satisfaction.
  Ah, the shifting of one's worldview to match the group's
  worldview.
  To quote from Robert Lanza, M.D., author of Biocentrism:
  "There is no invisible matrix out there that explains our
  origin. Rather, for each life there is a universe, its own
  universe.
  According to biocentrism, each of us generates our own sphere of
  reality. (We carry space and time around us like turtles with
  shells.) The Universe is comprised of billions of sphere of
  reality, a melange whose scope is breathtaking. Strikingly,
  anything you don't observe directly exists only as potential -
  or more mathematically speaking - as a haze of probability.
  Nothing exists until it is observed."

  It has always interested me how in any group therapy session -
  of which abduction groups are a part - start to 'feed' off each
  other's experience. As if by osmosis, a consensus forms, usually
  taking on the internal voice of the strongest personality in the
  group.
  Instead of guiding the individual into stepping back from
  whatever subjective experience they had and examine it using an
  objective mind. The ability to shift to the objective mind in
  the middle of an subjective experience requires a certain amount
  of rigorous self-training and learning to keep a balance between
  the two. Some Native American cultures taught their children to
  train this ability.
  Most white people I know, after reading the above, would wonder
  what the hell I am talking about. African- American children who
  grew up with "street smarts" probably have an idea.

  KK

  >>Date: Thu, 17 Jun 2010 13:57:36 +0100
  >>Subject: Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?
  ><snip>
  >>We could either be glimpsing alien activities or we might just
  >>be chasing 'alien fog' generated by our own vivid imaginations
  >>and a strong belief that we are not alone in the universe.
  >Radar and cameras don't imagine things.

  Eleanor:
  I have been reading an interesting book called The Wizards Of
  Langley by Jeffrey Richelson. One bit is confirming a story I
  heard from a retired Grumman engineer about his research into a
  device that collected radar signals, copied them/recorded them,
  had the ability to manipulate someone else's projected radar
  signal.
  The most astonishing part of the story was when Gene Poteat -
  project manager for the CIA's Palladium - was "testing" a system
  for creating a "ghost blip". T
  he TSS was monitoring the Cuban Air Force; 'painted' a 'ghost'
  aircraft on one of the Cuban pilot's radar. The Palladium
  operator made the blip do all kinds of flips, speed ups, zig-
  zags. The Cuban pilot claimed he was gaining on the aircraft. An
  aircraft he could not spot visually. As the pilot got almost
  next to the blip, Poteat nodded to the operator to flip the
  switch to off. Of course, the 'ghost aircraft' disappeared into
  thin air.
  After reading the above cited book, I now know the name of the
  project and the author repeats the same story about the Cuban
  pilot.
  An aside: I met Poteat in social situation. A guy who held his
  cards very close to his chest.

  KK

                                                      Classic TV's Take On UFOs
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Sat, 19 Jun 2010 01:09:22 EDT
  Archived: Sat, 19 Jun 2010 07:38:48 -0400
  Subject: Classic TV's Take On UFOs

  Sometimes you've just got to take a step back and laugh at this
  UFO biz or you'll go crazy.
  I found a gem of a comical look at the UFO phenom in one of the
  classic TV episodes of the 1960's show Green Acres.
  http://tinyurl.com/2ea2uga
  [US viewers only --ebk]
  This show was about a prominent NY lawyer who makes the error of
  romanticizing about the farming lifestyle and finds himself a
  real fish out of water. He's constantly at odds with his rural
  neighbors and many a misunderstanding leads to frustration and
  lots of laughs.
  It starred the best of the best in show biz and comedy and in
  this particular episode it takes on the issue of UFOs.
  It's as timely now as it was when it was broadcast 40 years ago.
  You can actually watch the whole episode for free by clicking
  the above link. Best if you have a broadband connection. If
  you don't end up howling with laughter after watching this
  program, you're taking this UFO stuff too seriously.

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com

                                   Re: Roscoe Hillenkoetter & His NICAP Involvement
  From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  Date: Sat, 19 Jun 2010 21:38:16 +0000
  Archived: Sat, 19 Jun 2010 19:18:04 -0400
  Subject: Re: Roscoe Hillenkoetter & His NICAP Involvement

  A long read, but hopefully, tons of info. The following is
  informed by The Wizards of Langley, by Jeffrey Richelson.
  Hillenkoetter became head of a new agency - renamed from Central
  Intelligence Group to Central Intelligence Agency. During his
  first few years, he fought to control the resources of what was
  called the Scientific Branch. The Scientific Branch had been
  part of the real MJ-12. A "working group" formally known as the
  Joint Research and Development Board headed by Dr. Vannevar
  Bush. Eventually this aspect of CIA's purview became the TSS
  (Technical Services Staff, and finally the TSD - Technical
  Services Division). This meant Hillenkoetter signed off on and
  was in charge of various projects.
  For exmaple:
  OXCART - project which included U-2 and other exotic aircraft
  over flights of enemy territory.
  MELODY & PALLADIUM - technology to control, copy, create,
  manipulate radar frequencies.
  And my particular interest, the mind control programs. Starting
  with BLUEBIRD, which Hillenkoetter changed the name to ARTICHOKE
  as he changed the research directives. Eventually, ARTICHOKE
  evolved into MKULTRA and MKDELTA. Hillenkoetter retired publicly
  in October of 1950.
  He surfaces again in 1956 when he offers to become a member of
  the Board of Governors of the National Investigations Committee
  on Aerial Phenomena (NICAP). The following is informed by Above
  Top Secret, Timothy Good, author. (Good disclosed that
  Hillenkoetter was a former Director of the CIA, but not much
  else.)
  Hillenkoetter was a board member of NICAP for a much longer
  period of time than he was DCI of the CIA. Good quoted from
  Hillenkoetter's statement - in a letter to Congress in August,
  1961 - his reasons UFOs were a threat (I quote in part): "1. The
  risk of accidental war from mistaking UFO formations for a
  Soviet attack. 2. The danger that the Soviet government may, in
  a critical moment, falsely claim the UFOs as secret Russian
  weapons against which our defenses are helpless."
  In 1962, Hillenkoetter surprises everybody and resigns saying:
  "I know the UFOS are not U.S. or Soviet devices. All we can do
  now is wait for some action by the UFOs. The Air Force cannot do
  any more under the circumstances. It has been a difficult
  assignment for them, and I believe we should not continue to
  criticize their investigations." Hillenkoetter had made his
  pronouncement. Was it what he really believed? I think, like a
  good disinfo officer, he sent everybody down a rabbit's hole. Do
  I have documentation? No, but over the years I am skeptical of
  statements by such a person as the DCI of the CIA.
  Personally, I know that anyone in intelligence never retires. I
  have met face-to-face NSA employees who thought they had retired
  only to be brought back into service.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m19-020.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:20]
Re: Roscoe Hillenkoetter & His NICAP Involvement

  Hillenkoetter wasn't the only high level retired CIA official on
  NICAP's board. According to Good, another board member was
  Joseph Bryan III. He had been the original Chief of
  Psychological Warfare Staff at the CIA under Hillenkoetter.
  Bryan joined NICAP in 1960. While at NICAP Bryan made a
  statement to the effect that UFO reports were being "officially
  withheld ." He doesn't say why, but I imagine he was thinking
  issues of national security. But, not for the reasons most UFO
  researchers would imagine.
  Even back in the days when the American public had very little
  knowledge of where their tax dollars were going, Major Donald
  Keyhoe, head of NICAP, had his suspicions about these two
  individuals' interest in NICAP's directive.
  I'm with Keyhoe. Based on my research into mind control projects
  and how they work: 1) A target audience/group/individual is
  selected and the concept/event choosen. 2) The catchnet program
  monitors the target's reaction to the impetus. Both
  Hillenkoetter and Bryan placed themselves in an ideal position
  to monitor how the public was reacting to the various "tests"
  the CIA was conducting in the public eye. Tests not necessarily
  for the benefit of the target, but there would be visuals of
  strange things flying around in the sky.
  Richard Dolan, in his book UFOs and the National Security State,
  begins to bring up the obvious overlap between deliberate mind
  control programs, and the infiltration of groups by both
  government and private corporations looking to monitor public
  opinion/perception in the UFO culture. Excellent book.
  My bottomline? Yes, there is "real" phenomena. And, yes, there
  is deliberate attempts to interfere by powerful agencies - both
  private and public. They might be interfering because they think
  they are protecting humanity. From what? I doubt if even "they"
  know.
  And, the beat of the hardline UFO researchers goes on insisting
  it's nuts and bolts craft. The biggest cover-up is that we
  humans are perpetrating a hoax on ourselves. It is time to step
  back, broaden the worldview and check out other separate by
  equal realities. Perhaps, a different reality just next door to
  ours.

  If you have gotten to the end of this e-mail,
  thanks for your patience,
  KK

  military witnesses _are_among_ the best witnesses on a
  _collective_ basis, due to their training, defensive military
  practices, and generally more on-duty or alert perspective
  and on-guard status, whether pilots or ground personnel, due to
  their normal responsibilities to protect our nation's security.
  Even so, he's voicing his opinion if what you say is true. I'd
  like to see a quote to bolster your contentions, however, rather
  than simply taking your word for it, as you are at least
  somewhat biased, apparently, based on your comments above and
  previ- ously. We don't need any more conflict, personality wars,
  or extremist viewpoints in ufology than we have now or than
  there has been, as that is mutually self-defeating.
  Why not, as gentlemen, and hopefully gentlewomen, we all try to
  agree to disagree at times, and attempt to see what kinds of
  mutual agreements, hypotheses, and research we can share among
  ourselves, in turn, rather than play these silly, juvenile games
  of "well, I disagree with you, and so therefore you should leave
  the 'playing field' or get out of my way."
  Let's try to exercise a bit more discipline, tolerance, and
  wisdom, OK? Otherwise the circus sideshow and infinitely
  regressive negative feedback loop will never end, and research
  and analysis will be neglected in these perpetual, arrogant
  battles.
  Vallee has said many times and discussed with me personally that
  the reason he longer engages in public debate or attempts to
  argue his findings and viewpoint is because of this type of
  "political" or personal, ad hominem approach and "warfare."
  His general public absence is our loss, as a result. He still
  does private research, and says the best thing serious, young
  research- ers can do in this field, such as it is and has
  become, is to learn how to do objective, empirical, truly
  scientific field investigation and case pattern-analyses and
  comparisons. After over 45 years of his devotion to and
  visionary breakthroughs in considering the ufo subject,
  particularly the inherent self-limitations regarding the
  standard ETH, I think we all might take heed and learn the
  lesson of his advanced education, long-term experience, and
  open-minded, sophisticated intelligence and perspective views.
  If not, then in my humble opinion, it is rather more likely that
  it will actually turn out to be "bye, bye, Kathy" than the other
  way around. My sincere hope, as you grow older and if you put
  the time and effort into the trenches of real endeavor to
  investigate the ufo phenomenon, is that you too may eventually
  learn just how true what I'm saying and suggesting is. Your
  mileage may vary [YMMV], but I'm neither a youngster nor a
  "geezer" - I'm somewhere in the great middle - but I've been
  around, done field research on cases, both new and old, public
  and unknown, and have had a couple close encounters myself, so
  I'm no "armchair ufologist." People like Vallee and Sparks have
  even thanked me for researching and documenting some historical
  issues and controversies they said I had made new discoveries
  about and illuminated some facts regarding same.

  But this is usually the kind of argument nobody "wins" and makes
  all who so participate in this "tit for tat" manner end up
  looking like losers in such gamesmanship. But sometimes you do
  have to fight fire with fire, preferably of a more subtle but
  firm kind, in order to try to educate the unruly. However, if
  those who want to up the ante persist, I'm prepared only to go
  so far, and then walk away, as there really is no intelligent
  alternative when some people just want to provoke or insult
  others. What's the point? It's completely inappropriate and
  unproductive, IMHO.
  As Rodney King once famously said, "People, I just want to say,
  you know, can we all get along?" And if not, perhaps we should
  try, and if not, agree to disagree, or ignore the naysayers and
  provocateurs, whose purpose and intent is simply to muddy the
  waters, stir the pot, and impede real progress, research and
  honest analysis.
  In our great republic, and based on the true principles of demo-
  cracy, we don't need or want censorship, but _more_ speech, more
  varied approaches, and more real, multi-disciplinary
  investigation whose results and findings should be more readily
  and openly shared to advance the field beyond mere bickering and
  game-playing. 'Nuff said. For now. Please think about it.

  Steve

  "contactees" such as Meier, Vorilhon, and Strieber, for example,
  in their supposed documentation and writings. We would all
  (well, maybe some of us) like to know The Pelicanist's opinion
  on these august matters. 6^}
