Rev Paul Eric Inglesby - 1915-2010




                                                          UFO Updates
                                     A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                 'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 20

                                                 Rev Paul Eric Inglesby - 1915-2010
  From: David Clarke <daveclarke292.nul>
  Date: Sun, 20 Jun 2010 10:59:33 +0100
  Archived: Sun, 20 Jun 2010 07:15:58 -0400
  Subject: Rev Paul Eric Inglesby - 1915-2010

  One of Britain's longest serving Christian UFO theorists, Rev
  Paul Inglesby has died aged 94 years at Glastonbury in Somerset.
  Inglesby's interest in UFOs began at the dawn of the modern UFO
  era in 1947 and he was one of the first subscribers to the
  magazine Flying Saucer Review in 1955.
  During the war he served under Lord Mountbatten at Combined
  Operations, Whitehall, and the two men continued to exchange
  correspondence on UFOs post-war.
  For a full obituary and links to my Fortean Times article
  covering Inglesby's Christian interpretation of of UFOs as
  demonic signs, see my blog news page.
  --
  David Clarke
  http://drdavidclarke.blogspot.com/
  http://www.drdavidclarke.co.uk/

Unexplained Phenomena Announces UFO Reports




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 20

                                     Unexplained Phenomena Announces UFO Reports
  From: Eustaquio Andrea Patounas <socex.ufobras.nul>
  Date: Sun, 20 Jun 2010 09:34:02 -0300
  Archived: Sun, 20 Jun 2010 12:08:56 -0400
  Subject: Unexplained Phenomena Announces UFO Reports

  Source: PRWeb
  http://www.prweb.com/releases/2010/06/prweb4162584.htm
  June 20, 2010
  Bob Bain of Unexplained Phenomena, LLC announced with great
  excitement of the groundbreaking new UFO Report Database powered
  by a solid leader in database software called IP.Content;
  otherwise known as CCS. Once completed the UFO Reports Database
  will go back through the entirety of the 1900's up to the current
  date.
  Lebanon, TN (PRWEB) June 20, 2010 -- Bob Bain of Unexplained
  Phenomena, LLC (http://unexplainedphenomena.net) announced with
  great excitement of the groundbreaking new UFO Report Database
  powered by a solid leader in database software called IP.Content;
  otherwise known as CCS. Once completed the UFO Reports Database
  will go back through the entirety of the 1900's up to the current
  date. This one of a kind Database has been sorely missed and
  desired by many UFO watchers, which up until now had no true
  database to report their sightings or experiences to.
  Besides for allowing sightings and abductions to be reported, the
  UFO Report Database allows other individuals the right to
  correspond with the original reporter of the incident.
  Unregistered users are fully welcomed by the Unexplained
  Phenomena Community. It is believed by management that
  unregistered users have much to offer. Mr. Bain Stated, "If the
  UFO Report Database denied reporting privileges to unregistered
  users then the Database would be missing a large portion of the
  UFO sighting spectrum".
  The Unexplained Phenomena Community as a whole is a group of
  dedicated individuals, investigators and sometimes Skeptics of
  various fields of the Unexplained. They take seriously their role
  as a purveyor of information to those who require a professional
  community to discuss topics, report incidents, which are
  generally seen as being totally outside the box of normality. It
  is the opinion of the founder that science is 70% theory and 30%
  factual. Bob Bain claims, "Scientists and hardcore Skeptics alike
  enjoy placing every Unexplained Phenomenon into the same type of
  box; the kind they can easily explain away. The problem is that
  the easiest answers to questions are more than likely the wrong
  answers to the presented question".
  It is true that science is an ever revolving door of theories.
  What once was thought science fiction one day becomes science
  factual the next. Science has answers only for a short period of
  time, and then the answers change as our knowledge-base
  expansively grows to new heights.
  "UFOs are real, there are ours and then there are theirs." Mr.
  Bain continues, "Ours, I believe was reversed engineered from
  theirs. As we further our advancements into reverse engineering
  their craft - the more advanced our craft will become. When I
  asked my cousin who at that time was a high ranking Air Force
  official about his knowledge of UFOs', explaining the things I
  personally have witnessed, he said "I can't speak about ours""


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m20-009.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:24]
Unexplained Phenomena Announces UFO Reports


  "Theirs, to quote my cousin who is now a retired Air Force
  official "they're crazy", and knowing him as I do - he meant it
  as in "Crazy technology, wild, advanced technology". But he was
  not allowed to really talk about it beyond giving small hints
  that a person would have to decode. Even to this day he can't
  speak on what he worked on, or seen."
  Besides for the UFO Reports, the Report Center currently houses
  Report Database for Ghost Stories and Dream Reports. You can
  visit the UFO Report Center by going to:
  http://UnexplainedPhenomena.net
  Report Center and choosing which of the report databases you wish
  to view, or click this direct link:
  http://unexplainedphenomena.net/reportcenter
  The main Report Center page will soon be updated to reflect how
  many reports each have and will also list the latest submitted
  report.

  Hi Don,
  Monotremes! Evolved monotremes: Monotremes (from the Greek monos
  'single' + trema 'hole', referring to the cloaca) are mammals
  that lay eggs (Prototheria) instead of giving birth to live young
  like marsupials (Metatheria) and placental mammals (Eutheria).
  They are not "Lizard people". They are mammals, just like humans,
  except for a few minor differences, which will probably take a while
  to get used to.

http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m21-003.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:26]
Re: What Good Use Is Back Engineering?

  with related as nothing short of terrifying - not benevolant
  space brothers the latter which seem to be here to warn us about
  a series of Earth threatening problems which they seem to be
  incapable of forecasting any faster then we ourselves can.
  Pick a generatiom and you get a new set of warnings. Nuclear
  destruction, polution, global warning. Ever notice that the
  space brothers always seem to come up with this stuff after we
  already know the potentials?
  Where were the forecasts of Global warming back in the 1950s by
  the then benevolent space brothers?
  To me this is akin to asigning a messiah similarity to a
  phenomenon and a quasi religious taint to what is obviously a
  very distrubing event in many peoples lives. They are either
  coming to grips with it by turning the abduction into a
  religious experience, thereby turning a really grungy and
  deeply dibilitating experience into an ego centric event they
  can live with and where they are the center of attention ( be
  the first on your block to be the new virgin Mary) or they are
  just plain lying about what is happening to them to begin with.
  I don't assign motives to the other side of the coin, however.
  Maybe some of claims of cruelty of these entities by abductees
  have to be looked at in a broader context. One being, they don't
  care what you are feeling any more than we care what a rat
  thinks while we are experimenting on them. It's a detachment of
  one species from the feelings of another. Maybe they don't have
  the same emotional bent that we do. If humans weren't so
  emotional we would likely have less hatred in this world and
  likely fewer wars. Territorality is another consideration-but
  that still comes down to an emotional attachment to place driven
  by comfort, availability of resources and food.
  But of course these people will - like the Adamski's of old -
  become part of the fabric of the abduction scenario and thereby
  taint it (like dog poop stuck to a shoe) increasing the
  difficulty of getting help with looking at this from the so-
  called professionals. Hopefully not the likes of those such as
  Casey.

  Don Ledger

                                                Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  From: Paul Scott Anderson <paulscottanderson.nul>
  Date: Mon, 21 Jun 2010 20:39:10 -0700
  Archived: Tue, 22 Jun 2010 07:36:24 -0400
  Subject: Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past

  >From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>
  >Date: Fri, 11 Jun 2010 12:49:25 -0300
  >Subject: Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past
  >I suggest to you that trace case 'object' landings were
  >hi-jacked in the 1980s and the Crop Circle clones were born-by
  >hoaxters. I'd be more interested in seeing some separation of
  >these circular impressions from the stylized crop circles. By
  >this I mean the exploded nodules etc, are they found in plain
  >circles that could be a depression made by some landed object or
  >are they found in the more artistictic formations? To what
  >degree?
  >Since microwave evidence pops up in association with the UAP
  >phenomenon - I see a lot in the aviation vein - I can see
  >vegetation damage done by same when one of these set down on the
  >ground. I have no problem with this-and I never have. Ted
  >Phillips researched many hundreds of these cases over the years.
  >See:
  >http://www.angelfire.com/mo/cptr/
  >Damage is done to the ground as I mentioned in an earlier
  >posting. The literature is full of damage to grass that doesn't
  >recover over the years-where even snow won't stay on top of the
  >impression.
  >The pretty crop circles seem less attractive to me the more
  >intricate they get. Give me an old fashioned 'saucer nest' any
  >time.
  As I tried to show you, there are reports of _other_ circles and
  rings which are _not_ the same as landing trace cases, but more
  like contemporary crop circles. Circles with sharp edges with
  standing centres of bunches of stalks. Rings, spirals, squares,
  etc. Did you read the Staffordshire (1686) and Nature (1880)
  reports I linked to? And those are just two. The Staffordshire
  ones in particular _were_ stylized geometric designs, albeit
  fairly simple ones, documented by the chemistry professor at
  Oxford. They may be an entirely separate phenomenon from UFO
  landing traces. Why not?
  The "exploded nodules" have been found in both simple circles
  and other designs. I helped document the ones in large cattle
  corn stalks a few years ago near me, among others, all on the
  CCCRN web site. They were all in large geometric designs. What
  about my point of some being found in _still-standing_ stalks
  around the perimeters of circles, but not farther out in the
  field. As I said before also, I've looked at other stalks that
  had been flattened in tests and exposed to the sun for some
  time, with no effect on the nodes at all. How does all that that
  square with your idea that they are just the result of flattened
  stalks laying out in the sun? It doesn't. Not all formations
  have them though of course, and offhand I can't think of any
  known man- made formations, such as commissioned ones, that were
  found to have them. There may be another prosaic cause, but that
  is still unknown as yet.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m22-005.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:30]
Re: Crop Circles - Back To The Past


  I've said publicly that I think many of the current formations
  are man-made, many more than there used to be, but there is a
  core genuine phenomenon, just as with UFOs. People started
  making their own, and progressively more elaborate over the
  years, but that does not take away from the original phenomenon.
  That's also why I am still largely interested in simpler
  formations while a lot of people are only interested in the big
  fancy ones.
  Any input here from Kathy K?

  Paul

  Replying to Kathy and Nigel -
  Yes, I understand fully that cameras and radar are capable of
  artifacts and manipulation. But can it be assumed that _all_
  photos and radar are artifacts or manipulation?
  There are cases where radar confirms what witnesses report, in
  the same area at the same time. Even more convincing are the
  multiple radar/multiple witness sightings.
  So for all their known shortcomings, one thing cameras and radar
  don't do is "imagine" things, particularly when their images are
  confirmed by witnesses.

  Eleanor White

  >I've said publicly that I think many of the current formations
  >are man-made, many more than there used to be, but there is a
  >core genuine phenomenon, just as with UFOs. People started
  >making their own, and progressively more elaborate over the
  >years, but that does not take away from the original phenomenon.
  >That's also why I am still largely interested in simpler
  >formations while a lot of people are only interested in the big
  >fancy ones.
  >Any input here from Kathy K?
  Paul:
  I used to enjoy looking at each year's new bunch of crop
  circles. I would even order the Crop Circle calendar from
  Strieber's On-Line Book Store. Just like you, I wondered whether
  at least one crop circle was created by some unknown phenomena.
  However, since the interest in crop circles has turned to
  attaching meaning to them that is not supported by the internal
  integrity of the design, I am afraid - for me - they have lost
  their charm.

  KK

  "No it was definitely a UFO which changed shape"
  ---

  KK after her posts here.
  I could be wrong and she could be a lovely young lady, but
  nothing I have read here, gives me that opinion.

  From: Scott Corrales <lornis1.nul>
  Date: Thu, 24 Jun 2010 06:51:44 -0400
  Archived: Thu, 24 Jun 2010 10:32:49 -0400
  Subject: Peruvian Airliner Passenger Photographs UFO

  The Journal of Hispanic Ufology
  June 24, 2010

  Peru: Airliner Passenger Photographs UFO
  Source: Con Nuestro Peru:
  http://connuestrperu.com
  and Planeta UFO
  Date: June 24, 2010
  Peru: Airliner Passenger Photographs UFO
  An Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) was photographed during a
  commercial flight over Peru.
  The object was seen in broad daylight on June 18th during a TACA
  flight headed from Lima to Piura.
  The object is visible along with some detail of the aircraft's
  wing. Around 3:00 p.m., one of the TACA passengers saw the
  object beyond one of the wings.
  Not believeing what he was seeing, the passenger alerted other
  travelers, and was able to ascertain that they were seeing the
  same thing.
  He then reached for his camera and took these photos, which were
  turned over to Con Nuestro Peru
  The passenger is a renowned attorney who requested
  confidentiality.
  [Aside from the considerable quality of the images, and the
  corroboration by fellow passengers, the fact that the Piura
  region has become known for its luminous phenomena is also
  noteworthy. See "The Piura Lights" entry earlier this year in
  INEXPLICATA -- SC]

  Translation (c) 2010, S. Corrales, IHU

Re: Aliens & Imagination


  >Hope the above clarifies things.

  The fact that KK doesn't know what RB-47 is confirms to me that
  she is a lightweight - if that - in this field. Her misdirected
  information about Palladium seems to reinforce this as well.
  Palladium was a three part utilization of covert radar signals,
  released reflecting agents and Soviet radar reception.
  KK mentioned the "'air force" bases being constructed in Cuba -
  and there were a few - but the Cuban missile crises involved
  Soviet manned missile sites not bases. Palladium was used to
  determine - in one experimen for example - if Soviet radar could
  detect the A-12/SR-71/Oxcart during its fly-over of secret
  Soviet territory. Despite early attempts at making the A-12
  stealthy - its radar signature small and insignificat -
  Palladium tests proved that the Soviets would see it with their
  radar.
  It worked this way.
  A navy vessel was situated just over the horizon from Havanna,
  Cuba. It's radar antenna however peeked over the horizon. An
  American sub stole into Havanna Bay and sufaced just enough to
  release graduated varying sizes of floating reflective balls
  from smallest to largest.
  The US navy ship broadcast a calibrated radar beam in that
  direction which reflected off the floating spheres and was
  picked up - passively - by the SA-2 Soviet radar the Cubans were
  using. The Cubans then switched to ranging mode and started
  beaming active radar pulses at the spheres thinking the mass of
  floating spheres were an airplane. It was at what point and size
  of the spheres that the SA-2 radar detected the spheres that was
  the point of the experiment.
  The SA-2 proved to be good and picked the relatively small
  spheres which the experiment then proved that the Soviets would
  also be able to detect the signature of the A-12 or codenamed
  Oxcart. Palladium soon went by the wayside as too cumbersome to
  operate. It was also deemed that the Soviets would be able to do
  the same thing and the project was dropped.

  Don Ledger

                                            Vallee's Summary On Crop Circles - Pt - 4
  From: Steve Sawyer <stevesaw.nul>
  Date: Thu, 24 Jun 2010 22:29:24 -0700
  Archived: Fri, 25 Jun 2010 06:30:16 -0400
  Subject: Vallee's Summary On Crop Circles - Pt - 4

  Hi List,
  Here is Jacques Vallee's summary Part Four of his series of
  posts on boingboing.com concerning crop circles, and whether
  they are man-made, related to the intelligence community, or
  other sources, and their potential use as memetic psyops of
  varying kinds possibly involving the use and manipulation of the
  internet as a means of transmission:
  Of Crop Circles, meme wars, and web-based flypaper
  http://bit.ly/aXtYmd
  (These are the titles of the three prior posts by Vallee on the
  subject of crop circles that appeared on boingboing):
  1. In Search of Alien Glyphs (or are they microwave blasters?)
  http://bit.ly/aEPSKX
  2. Of Flattened Flora and Expulsion Cavities: The crop circle
  controversy continues
  http://bit.ly/d1XCGB
  3. Of Flattened Flora and Expulsion Cavities: The crop circle
  controversy continues
  http://bit.ly/biWPqN
  Of at least as much interest as Vallee's posts are the comment
  threads following each post from readers. Vallee did not reply
  in any of the comment threads to some readers questions as to
  why, if some kind of microwave, maser, or other EMR type beam
  radiation is emitted from aerial, man-made platforms of any kind
  as Vallee speculates about, why the crops inside circles and
  related formations do not display or suffer radiation effects
  from the top of the plant stalks in differing crops where crop
  circles have been found (of the other than typical, common
  board- and-rope stomping kind), or why the crop so affected via
  this covert means allegedly related to the intelligence
  community as a form of psyops or "meme war" (and for what
  reasons?) do not bend flat from the top down, instead of as is
  virtually always seen, crops being bent over from the ground
  level.
  I found it frustrating that, despite this repeated comment and
  query, Vallee did not respond in any of the four part post
  series to readers questions, in either subsequent crop circle
  posts for boingboing or in the related comment threads. Not
  really very satisfactory to ignore or avoid at least responding
  to this quite relevant question in relation to Vallee's alleged
  hypothesis and speculations, IMHO.

  Steve

                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                     [Corrected] Vallee's Summary On Crop Circles - Pt
  From: Steve Sawyer <stevesaw.nul>
  Date: Fri, 25 Jun 2010 12:03:58 -0700
  Archived: Sat, 26 Jun 2010 06:59:52 -0400
  Subject: [Corrected] Vallee's Summary On Crop Circles - Pt

  Hi,
  This is a modified/corrected version of my prior post, which
  includes a correction to the list of three prior articles. The
  title of the third was duplicated by error, and it includes
  supplementary commentary.
  ---
  Hi, List
  Here is Jacques Vallee's summary Part Four of his series of
  posts on BoingBoing.com concerning crop circles, and whether
  they are man-made, related to the intelligence community, or
  other sources, and their potential use as memetic psyops of
  varying kinds possibly involving the use and manipulation of the
  internet as a means of transmission:
  Of Crop Circles, meme wars, and web-based flypaper
  http://bit.ly/aXtYmd
  (These are the titles of the three prior posts by Vallee on the
  subject of crop circles that appeared on BoingBoing):
  1. In Search of Alien Glyphs (or are they microwave blasters?)
  http://bit.ly/aEPSKX
  2. Crop Circles, Part Deux: Alien Glyphs, Human Myths, Blogging
  Bliss
  http://bit.ly/d1XCGB
  3. Of Flattened Flora and Expulsion Cavities: The crop circle
  controversy continues
  http://bit.ly/biWPqN
  Of at least as much interest as Vallee's posts are the comment
  threads following each post from readers. Vallee did not reply
  in any of the comment threads to some readers questions as to
  why, if some kind of microwave, maser, or other EM-type beam
  radiation is emitted from aerial, man-made platforms of any
  kind, as Vallee speculates about, why the crops inside circles
  and related formations do not display or suffer radiation
  effects from the _top_ of the plant stalks in differing crops
  where crop circles have been found (of the other than typical,
  common board-and-rope stomping kind), or why the crop so
  affected via this covert means allegedly related to the
  intelligence community as a form of psyops or "meme war" (and
  for what reasons?) do not bend flat from the top down, instead
  of as is virtually always seen, crops being bent over from the
  _ground_ level.
  What, in terms of the physics or mechanics of radiation emitted
  from above, can explain the bending at the bottom of the stalks,
  not the top and/or throughout the plant stem if subjected to EM
  radiation from an aerial source?


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m26-002.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:42]
[Corrected] Vallee's Summary On Crop Circles - Pt


  I found it frustrating that, despite this repeated comment and
  query, Vallee did not respond in any of the four-part post-
  series to readers' questions, in either subsequent crop circle
  posts for BoingBoing or in the related comment threads. Not
  really very satisfactory to ignore or avoid at least responding
  to this quite relevant question in relation to Vallee's alleged
  hypothesis and speculations, IMHO, even though Vallee makes it
  clear he read all the readers comments.
  As one reader noted (hungryjoe, comment #4) ref. part four's
  concluding post:
  "The skeptical audience here on BoingBoing probably won't have a
  fruitful discussion of the misinformation campaign surrounding
  crop circles. This is because we are ardently doubtful about the
  initial proposition. "To summarize that position: the shadowy
  military industrial complex has been testing a crop-damaging
  (but not destroying) beam weapon for decades by etching
  whimsical designs in farmers' fields.
  "We would all have to be on board with this concept in order to
  then believe and discuss the misinformation aspect."
  Perhaps the answer to the question is inexplicable, or the
  answer simply unknown, but I thought the question deserved
  _some_ kind of response, even if Vallee could only say, "I don't
  know the mechanism involved" (in only the bottom portion of the
  crops being affected rather than the top or throughout the
  stalks, which causes the plants to bend over creating elaborate
  patterns), or something equivalent.
  ---

Russian Article Mentions Robert Hasting's Work




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 26

                                      Russian Article Mentions Robert Hasting's Work
  From: Robert Hatings <ufohastings.nul>
  Date: Sat, 26 Jun 2010 14:25:30 -0400 (EDT)
  Archived: Sat, 26 Jun 2010 20:43:28 -0400
  Subject: Russian Article Mentions Robert Hasting's Work

  All,
  Last week, a large-circulation Russian newspaper, Life,
  mentioned one of the nukes-related UFO cases that I investigated
  some years ago. I subsequently posted an article of my own, at
  UFO Chronicles, in which I translated the Russian one, utilizing
  the assistance of the reporter, Inessa Kornienko. My article is
  attached. Perhaps you will find it of interest.
  Robert
  ---
  Recent Russian Newspaper Article Discusses UFO Incidents at
  Soviet and American Nuclear Weapons Sites
  6-21-10
  By Robert Hastings
  Persons familiar with my work know that I investigate nuclear
  weapons-related UFO activity. Over the past 37 years, I have
  interviewed more than 120 former or retired U.S. military
  personnel who were involved in UFO incidents at nuclear missile
  sites, weapons storage depots, strategic bomber bases, or
  atmospheric test sites in Nevada and the Pacific. Many of my
  findings are available at my website, www.ufohastings.com. A
  more comprehensive summary may be found in my 600-page book UFOs
  and Nukes: Extraordinary Encounters at Nuclear Weapons Sites.
  Following the collapse of the Soviet Union in 1991, a number of
  ex-Soviet Army personnel came forward and began discussing their
  involvement in similar incidents in that country during the Cold
  War era. One of those events occurred on October 4, 1982, near
  the Ukrainian town of Byelokoroviche, when a disc-shaped UFO
  apparently hovered over a nuclear missile base for an extended
  period. At one point during the encounter, a number of nuclear
  missiles suddenly activated-without authorization from Moscow or
  any action being taken by the missile launch officers-and were
  preparing to launch! Had they done so, World War III would have
  very probably been underway. Fortunately, after 15 seconds, the
  anomalous activation ceased and the missiles returned to stand-
  by status. A subsequent investigation by the Soviet government
  discovered no equipment malfunctions that would have explained
  the event.
  This incident was first publicized in October 1994, on the
  American ABC News program Prime Time Live (which unethically
  used KGB documents and source leads provided to its producers by
  KLAS-TV reporter George Knapp, without crediting his
  contributions). A transcript of that program may be found here.
  More recently, on June 16, 2010, a large-circulation Russian
  newspaper, Life, published an article about the case, which may
  be found at:
  http://zhizn.ru/articles/show/192
  A badly-mangled English translation of the article, courtesy of


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m26-008.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:45]
Russian Article Mentions Robert Hasting's Work

  Google, is also available.
  One of the paper's reporters, Inessa Kornienko, interviewed
  hitherto unidentified witnesses who provided additional details
  about the incident. While preparing the article, Kornienko
  contacted me and asked if I were aware of the case. I told her
  that not only did I know about it, I had once interviewed a
  former U.S. Air Force nuclear missile launch officer, David H.
  Schuur, who had described a very similar event at Minot AFB,
  North Dakota, in the mid-1960s. A verbatim summary of Schuur's
  revelations may be found here.
  Kornienko incorporated some of my remarks in the Life article,
  and noted the obvious similarity between the two cases. Because
  Google's Russian-to-English translation is so torturous, and
  thoroughly incomprehensible at times, I asked the reporter for a
  few clarifications, which she provided, so that a more user-
  friendly translation might be achieved. The result follows here.
  ---
  The Life Article:
  UFO Hacked Nuclear Codes
  Russia and the USA were on the brink of nuclear war because of
  UFO attacks on military bases where nuclear missiles were on
  alert.
  Sensational documents and other evidence confirm that the Soviet
  military base near Byelokoroviche, Ukraine, and the U.S. Air
  Force base near Minot, North Dakota, were attacked by aliens
  from space. Hovering over the missiles, [in each incident,] a
  UFO in a matter of seconds hacked the codes required to launch
  them, nearly unleashing a Third World War. Journalists working
  for Life found revealing documents about [the Ukrainian
  incident] and located eyewitnesses to the alien attack.
  For a 20-year-old radio operator, Vladimir Matveyev, assigned to
  the 50th Missile Division RVSN, Carpathian Military District,
  October 4, 1982 was a day that he will remember for the rest of
  his life. In the evening, he and a thousand soldiers and
  officers saw a UFO for almost an hour, as it hovered over the R-
  12 missile silos. "It was unbelievable. Approximately one-and-a-
  half kilometers from us hovered an elliptical-shaped object,"
  the former rocketeer excitedly told Life. "The dimensions of the
  UFO shocked us-as large as a five-story house! Barely-visible
  lights flew up to the object. The guys [and I] were on our way
  to dinner when we all saw it! The UFO continued to hover, slowly
  moving to the left, as if drifting. One officer tried to get
  closer to it in a car but the UFO flew away. At this time all of
  the missile launchers malfunctioned. The UFO [also] blocked
  radio signal reception in the bunker. We heard only complete
  silence, which we could not understand, because this had never
  happened before. We were [later] told that the radio equipment
  was burnt!"
  The Report
  In his official statement on the incident, Major Michael
  Katzman, who was responsible for the missiles' guidance systems,
  reported that the computer equipment and security systems had
  been disabled by a powerful [electromagnetic] pulse. He wrote
  that all of the control panels had [suddenly] lit up, indicating
  the missiles were preparing to launch toward their strategic
  targets.
  Former TsSBUiS [missile division] Chief Yuri Zolotukhin told a
  Life journalist, "I too was a witness to these events and also
  saw the UFO, but could not reveal what had happened to the
  sensitive equipment because I signed a non-disclosure document
  [designed to] protect state secrets." These events happened in
  the underground bunker where the missile control panels display
  the missiles' readiness status. [During the incident] the panels
  lit up, indicating that the missiles had gone to full combat
  readiness and were preparing to launch. [Ordinarily] this is
  possible only after obtaining an order from Moscow. In this
  case, it happened by itself. The officers on duty at their
  battle stations were shocked. [Figuratively speaking,] their
  hair had turned gray. They said that the information appearing
  on the control panels indicated that all security measures


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m26-008.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:45]
Russian Article Mentions Robert Hasting's Work

  designed to prevent an unauthorized launch of the missiles had
  been hacked! Within just a few seconds, the launch officers had
  lost control over their nuclear weapons. Immediately after this
  occurred, the officers called Moscow. The reply they got was
  that no order to launch had been issued. After 15 seconds, all
  of the controls reset to the normal position.
  [Former] rocketeer Vladimir Matveyev says, "A few days later, a
  commission came to the base and interviewed the witnesses. The
  guys gave them their drawings of the UFO. One of the officers
  swore on his [Communist] Party membership that he wasn't drunk.
  A few days later we were lined up [for our morning inspection by
  our officers] and read an order from the Commander-in-Chief of
  the Strategic Rocket Forces, designated number R010, which said,
  'If you see a UFO, do not panic and do not shoot.' Then I
  realized why the officers who had their finger 'on the button'
  looked so old and had gray hair."
  USA
  A U.S. Air Force base in Minot, North Dakota, once experienced a
  similar incident, this Life journalist was told by American UFO
  investigator, 60-year-old Robert Hastings. He is the author of
  research on UFO activity at strategic nuclear facilities.
  Hastings personally spoke with a [former] Intercontinental
  Ballistic Missile commander, [then] Lieutenant David Schuur.
  "Schuur told me that in 1966 he was involved in an event in
  which an Unidentified Flying Object repeatedly activated [the
  launch sequence in his] missiles," says the ufologist. "Schuur
  told me that his missile guards had informed him that a big
  bright object was [moving from missile to missile]. When the UFO
  [hovered over] a [given] missile, his control panel indicated
  that it was preparing to launch. This meant that somehow it had
  received a launch authorization. Every time that happened,
  Schuur had to manually enter a launch 'Inhibit' command. The UFO
  seemed to be scanning the missiles [because various other
  functions were temporarily activated as well]. The next day,
  Schuur's commanders said that there was nothing to discuss."
  End Of Online Article
  In the hardcopy (print) article that Life circulated within
  Russia last week, additional material was published by the
  newspaper. Reporter/writer Kornienko sent me that version of the
  article in both Russian and Google-translated English. Among the
  comments appearing in it were these:
  My Take
  I think [the UFO] technology allows [their pilots] to launch our
  missiles. However, they choose not to do so. Cases such as
  Byelokoroviche and Minot [suggest] that aliens are trying to
  understand how these systems work, and what they need to do in
  the event of war breaking out, to stop the feuding children,
  i.e., us. I believe that the UFO [pilots], by running the
  rockets' pre-launch countdown, learned how to stop it.
  --Inessa Kornienko
     Life Reporter

  Any fan of aviation knows who 5 Star General Henry Hap Arnold
  was.
  Only 5 Star General to serve as head of the Army and Air Force.
  Not only was he instructed in flight by none other than the
  Wright Bros., but he practically invented our age of air power.
  Sure he was a protege of Gen. Billy Mitchell who pioneered air
  power but you can't take anything away from Gen. Arnold.
  Too bad he passed away just a few years after the Roswell
  Incident and I've wondered how much did he really know about
  UFOs then called Flying Saucers.
  I've read bits and pieces here and there but was wondering if
  anything new had come out.
  Any book references on him?

  Ufologiques organization to preview the major UFO Conference

  qualified visual and radar observers as well. Check it out on

  Greg and John,
  I believe that Hopkins, Jacobs and the late Dr. Mack all brought
  their own interpretation of the Alien Abduction phenomenon to
  the table, but certainly they shared notes and information to
  help validate their efforts. While there have been agreements,
  Jacob's view has been a bit darker in a general sense (IMO), but
  certainly they all have evolved their views over the years.
  Larry King would be an interesting venue, but not an earth
  shattering program that would spark a paradigm shift in beliefs.
  The audience would primarily be made up of those already
  involved in the genre, so I wouldn't hold my breath on a joint
  presentation having any impact. Larry is really just looking for
  a good audience and doesn't really have any dog in this fight,
  so to speak. He brings in the guests that will attract viewers
  and adds a resident skeptic to provide cover for the Network,
  and presents the information as a talk show host (not taking
  sides).
  The National Press Club is a venue that can be bought, so
  prestige and coverage by the media would be limited without
  promotion, but the recent on-line Symposium that was hosted
  there by Stephen Bassett may point to the future of UFO venues.
  But again, this is an audience that was already interested and
  involved.
  There was a time when the overnight programming on the Art Bell
  show was talked about extensively on various forums, such as
  UpDates, but George Noory doesn't seem to hold the same respect
  (although he may have greater on air talent). But that's

  Hmmm... is there something, John, about Oshkosh, Wisconsin, you
  know that we who have actually been there don't? I confess that
  I have not attended any meetings of its Rotary Club, though I've
  heard rumors that members have a grayish cast to their skin as
  well as unusually large eyes.
  >I believe that Hopkins, Jacobs and the late Dr. Mack all brought
  >their own interpretation of the Alien Abduction phenomenon to
  >the table, but certainly they shared notes and information to
  >help validate their efforts. While there have been agreements,
  >Jacobs' view has been a bit darker in a general sense (IMO), but
  >certainly they all have evolved their views over the years.
  The idea that Hopkins and Jacobs haven't fully communicated
  their ideas and evidence is crazy. A big public news conference
  would accomplish nothing, except to generate a fresh wave of
  ridicule on the phenomenon.
  The only occasion for a national press conference would be the
  announcement of conclusive, documented proof -- as opposed to a
  collection of anomalous testimonies which have settled nothing.
  Frankly, there are times when I wonder about the level of
  thought that goes into some (happily not all) of the postings on
  the List. Maybe it's a good idea to think things through a
  little before rushing to the keyboard.

  >There was a time when the overnight programming on the Art Bell

  Understood Steve, however my point is it's high past time that
  these two gentlemen, Hopkins and Jacobs to get their time in
  front of the world and state what their mutual results are
  regarding the abduction phenomenon.
  Since there's no way they can prove the ETH with physical
  evidence they can prove that people are and have been abducted
  and that experiments regarding several things primarily some
  form of breeding program has been going on.
  A friend in the know told me and I quote them as saying that
  history has shown this phenomenon since recorded history and
  that technically we can say angels are aliens but that aliens
  aren't necessarily angels.
  Its semantics but correct.
  I think after 60 plus years of the UFO phenom with no physical
  proof of the ETH, we should just look at the modus operandi that
  we can prove and tell the public the real deal. It may be
  sensational and indeed a paradigm shift or two but at least we
  can stand on something more than what our officials that we pay
  have refused to do.
  We've got so far tons of documentation, testimony from extremely
  credible witnesses and whistleblowers and victims, physical
  scars, objects extracted and medical records. We have trace
  cases but all a trace case says is that something interacted
  with something within the environment where a report took place.
  So again, all we can say is that persons utilizing advanced
  technologies have been kidnapping and experimenting on us and
  their technologies are so advanced in regard to stealth and
  evasion and coercion that we should be spending time and
  resources to thwart them. Even if these persons meant well, it
  doesn't show considering their obvious advanced resources.

Secrecy News - 06/28/10




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Jun > Jun 29

                                                          Secrecy News - 06/28/10
  From: Steven Aftergood <saftergood.nul>
  Date: Mon, 28 Jun 2010 12:31:45 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 29 Jun 2010 06:33:00 -0400
  Subject: Secrecy News - 06/28/10

  SECRECY NEWS
  from the FAS Project on Government Secrecy
  Volume 2010, Issue No. 53
  June 28, 2010
  Secrecy News Blog: http://www.fas.org/blog/secrecy/

  ** WIKILEAKS FAILS "DUE DILIGENCE" REVIEW

  WIKILEAKS FAILS "DUE DILIGENCE" REVIEW
  In the past week, both the Washington Post and the New York
  Times have referred to WikiLeaks.org, the web site that
  publishes confidential records, as a "whistleblower" site. This
  conforms to WikiLeaks' own instructions to journalists that
  "WikiLeaks should be described, depending on context, as the
  'open government group', 'anti-corruption group', 'transparency
  group' or 'whistleblower's site'."
  But calling WikiLeaks a whistleblower site does not accurately
  reflect the character of the project. It also does not explain
  why others who are engaged in open government, anti-corruption
  and whistleblower protection activities are wary of WikiLeaks or
  disdainful of it. And it does not provide any clue why the
  Knight Foundation, the preeminent foundation funder of
  innovative First Amendment and free press initiatives, might
  have rejected WikiLeaks' request for financial support, as it
  recently did.
  =46rom one perspective, WikiLeaks is a creative response to a real
  problem afflicting the U.S. and many other countries, namely the
  over-control of government information to the detriment of
  public policy. WikiLeaks has published a considerable number of
  valuable official records that had been kept unnecessarily
  secret and were otherwise unavailable, including some that I had
  attempted and failed to obtain myself. Its most spectacular
  disclosure was the formerly classified videotape showing an
  attack by a U.S. Army helicopter crew in Baghdad in 2007 which
  led to the deaths of several non-combatants. Before mostly going
  dormant late last year, it also published numerous documents
  that have no particular policy significance or that were already
  placed in the public domain by others (including a few that were
  taken from the FAS web site).
  WikiLeaks says that it is dedicated to fighting censorship, so a
  casual observer might assume that it is more or less a
  conventional liberal enterprise committed to enlightened
  democratic policies. But on closer inspection that is not quite
  the case. In fact, WikiLeaks must be counted among the enemies
  of open society because it does not respect the rule of law nor
  does it honor the rights of individuals.
  Last year, for example, WikiLeaks published the "secret ritual"
  of a college women's sorority called Alpha Sigma Tau. Now Alpha
  Sigma Tau (like several other sororities "exposed" by WikiLeaks)
  is not known to have engaged in any form of misconduct, and
  WikiLeaks does not allege that it has. Rather, WikiLeaks chose


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m29-001.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:50]
Secrecy News - 06/28/10

  to publish the group's confidential ritual just because it
  could. This is not whistleblowing and it is not journalism. It
  is a kind of information vandalism.
  http://wikileaks.org/wiki/The_secret_Ritual_of_Alpha_Sigma_Tau,_1975
  In fact, WikiLeaks routinely tramples on the privacy of non-
  governmental, non-corporate groups for no valid public policy
  reason. It has published private rites of Masons, Mormons and
  other groups that cultivate confidential relations among their
  members. Most or all of these groups are defenseless against
  WikiLeaks' intrusions. The only weapon they have is public
  contempt for WikiLeaks' ruthless violation of their freedom of
  association, and even that has mostly been swept away in a wave
  of uncritical and even adulatory reporting about the brave "open
  government," "whistleblower" site.
  On occasion, WikiLeaks has engaged in overtly unethical
  behavior. Last year, without permission, it published the full
  text of the highly regarded 2009 book about corruption in Kenya
  called "It's Our Turn to Eat" by reporter Michela Wrong (as
  first reported by Chris McGreal in The Guardian on April 9). By
  posting a pirated version of the book and making it freely
  available, WikiLeaks almost certainly disrupted sales of the
  book and made it harder for Ms. Wrong and other anti-corruption
  reporters to perform their important work and to get it
  published. Repeated protests and pleas from the author were
  required before WikiLeaks (to its credit) finally took the book
  offline.
  "Soon enough," observed Raffi Khatchadourian in a long profile
  of WikiLeaks' Julian Assange in The New Yorker (June 7),
  "Assange must confront the paradox of his creation: the thing
  that he seems to detest most - power without accountability - is
  encoded in the site=E2=80=99s DNA, and will only become more
  pronounced as WikiLeaks evolves into a real institution."

  http://www.newyorker.com/reporting/2010/06/07/100607fa_fact_khatchadourian
  Much could be forgiven to WikiLeaks if it were true that its
  activities were succeeding in transforming government
  information policy in favor of increased openness and
  accountability - as opposed to merely generating reams of
  publicity for itself. WikiLeaks supporter Glenn Greenwald of
  Salon.com wrote that when it comes to combating government
  secrecy, "nobody is doing that as effectively as WikiLeaks." But
  he neglected to spell out exactly what effect WikiLeaks has had.
  Which U.S. government programs have been cancelled as a result
  of Wikileaks' activities? Which government policies have been
  revised? How has public discourse shifted?
  (And, by the way, who has been injured by its work?)
  http://www.salon.com/news/opinion/glenn_greenwald/2010/06/18/wikileaks/
  A less sympathetic observer might conclude that WikiLeaks has
  squandered much of the impact that it might have had.
  A telling comparison can be made between WikiLeaks' publication
  of the Iraq Apache helicopter attack video last April and The
  New Yorker's publication of the Abu Ghraib abuse photographs in
  an article by Seymour Hersh in May 2004. Both disclosures
  involved extremely graphic and disturbing images. Both involved
  unreleased or classified government records. And both generated
  a public sensation. But there the similarity ends. The Abu
  Ghraib photos prompted lawsuits, congressional hearings, courts
  martial, prison sentences, declassification initiatives, and at
  least indirectly a revision of U.S. policy on torture and
  interrogation. By contrast, the WikiLeaks video tendentiously
  packaged under the title "Collateral Murder" produced none of
  that - no investigation (other than a leak investigation), no
  congressional hearings, no lawsuits, no tightening of the rules
  of engagement. Just a mild scolding from the Secretary of
  Defense, and an avalanche of publicity for WikiLeaks.
  Of course, it's hard for anyone to produce a specific desired
  outcome from the national security bureaucracy, and maybe
  WikiLeaks can't be faulted for failing to have done so. But with
  the whole world's attention at its command for a few days last
  April, it could have done more to place the focus on the victims
  of the incident that it had documented, perhaps even


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/jun/m29-001.shtml[10/12/2011 23:16:50]
Secrecy News - 06/28/10

  establishing a charitable fund to assist their families. But
  that's not what it chose to do. Instead, the focus remained
  firmly fixed on WikiLeaks itself and its own ambitious
  fundraising efforts.
  In perhaps the first independent review of the WikiLeaks
  project, the John S. and James L. Knight Foundation considered
  and rejected an application from WikiLeaks for financial
  support. The Knight Foundation was actively looking for grantees
  who could promote innovative uses of digital technology in
  support of the future development of journalism. At the end of
  the process, more than $2.7 million was awarded to 12 promising
  recipients. WikiLeaks was not among them.
  "Every year some applications that are popular among advisors
  don't make the cut after Knight staff conducts due diligence,"
  said Knight Foundation spokesman Marc Fest in response to an
  inquiry from Yahoo news. "WikiLeaks was not recommended by
  Knight staff to the board."
  http://news.yahoo.com/s/ynews/20100617/ts_ynews/ynews_ts2677_3

  >think they lord over everything. They're the ones who bring on

  >the wind and back in their faces.
  My point, Greg, was simply this:
  If you are going to announce something extraordinary and make a
  mountain of it, you had better have more than a molehill's worth
  of evidence. Like you, I find abduction accounts intriguing and
  puzzling. As with so many experience anomalies, however, the
  supporting evidence is overwhelmingly testimonial and no more.
  That is not the case with all UFO reports, of course (see for
  example Leslie Kean's forthcoming book), but it is true of
  abductees'. And I say that as one who was personally
  investigating abduction stories years before Budd Hopkins
  arrived on the scene -- and, I might add, long before I'd ever
  heard of Greg Boone.
  Any public endorsement of a fantastic assertion such as the
  reality of alien visitation requires much stronger validation
  than abduction cases, whatever their ultimate cause (which some
  anomalists suspect may not in fact be UFO-related), can provide.
  Let's put it this way: there's no abduction equivalent to, say,
  the RB-47 case.
  <snip>
  >>Frankly, there are times when I wonder about the level of
  >>thought that goes into some (happily not all) of the postings on
  >>the List. Maybe it's a good idea to think things through a
  >>little before rushing to the keyboard.
  >Well thanks for insulting (happily not all) Listers.
  If I had intended to insult anybody, I would have used far
  stronger language. Still, if you felt so, my apology. My
  intention was simply to put forth some friendly advice.

  >B means bomber as in B-17, B-24, B-29, B-36, B-52, B-58.
  >Some high performance B-47s were modified for Reconnaissance
  >missions.
  >The RB-47 case was a very high quality case lasting more than an
  >hour and involving air and ground radar and very highly
  >qualified visual and radar observers as well. Check it out on
  >Google

  Stan:
  I called some of you old guard - which _you_ have translated
  into old geezer. What would Freud have to say about that? Old
  guard does not have the same connotation as geezer. Apparently,
  you are still smarting over this. Please, please, I don't want
  to believe the entire field of ufo researchers is filled with
  individuals with low self-esteem.
  But, I have wonder why you want to make sure my opinion about
  the old guard is correct by making reference to being an old
  geezer.
  You could have simply said that I was stating truth to "power".
  However, I would have to put "power" in quotation because it is
  a wobbly throne all you geezers sit on. <LOL>

  From: Terry W. Colvin <fortean1.nul>
  Date: Wed, 30 Jun 2010 09:19:15 -0700 (GMT-07:00)
  Archived: Wed, 30 Jun 2010 12:35:35 -0400
  Subject: Gravity Anomalies

  [forteana] Gravity Anomalies
  Date: Jun 29, 2010 11:08 AM
  GOCE giving new insights into Earth's gravity
  http://www.esa.int/SPECIALS/GOCE/SEMY0FOZVAG_0.html
  29 June 2010
  The first global gravity model based on GOCE satellite
  data has been presented at ESA's Living Planet Symposium.
  ESA launched GOCE in March 2009 to map Earth's gravity
  with unprecedented accuracy and resolution.
  big version
  http://www.esa.int/images/GOCE-Geoidkarte_mit_Schatten_H.jpg
  ---
  As you can see, it looks like there's 'low gravity' in
  Canada (Hudson Bay), the coast of California, from the
  Bahamas down to Suriname (maybe including Puerto Rico)
  and an intense low-gravity area centered on Sri Lanka but
  extending north to the Himalayas.
  While the strongest 'high-gravity' areas are a) Northern
  Atlantic between Europe and Newfoundland, and b) all of Papua
  New Guinea and easterly as far as Fiji. Ray

  Terry W. Colvin
  North Las Vegas,
